# What remains to be implemented in DeluluLang

**Written:** 2026-08-23. **Method:** every markdown file in this repository was read — all 161 that
existed before this one — every feature claim in them was checked against the code, and every item
below was verified against the **current binary** rather than against what a document said. **Tree at the time of
writing:** 186 commits past the local `v1.0.0` tag, 13 workspace members (9 shipped language
crates, 4 repository tooling), 111,148 lines of Rust (2026-08-23), 154 registered diagnostic codes,
`delulu doctor` 17/17, Survey 0 errors.

> Every count in the line above is the Survey's, recounted from the tree. That is not a stylistic
> choice: the first draft of this file quoted **"308 registered diagnostic codes"**, taken from a
> grep of `codes.rs` that counted each code once per table and swept in the `UNALLOCATED` entries
> beside them. The Survey rejected it on the next build and named the real figure. A number in this
> repository that was not recounted is a number that is probably wrong — which is the argument §1
> of this document is largely made of.

**Updated 2026-11-06** by on-demand routine run 20: **4.95, 4.96 and 4.97 closed** (D-V2-210 to D-V2-212 — R-Get enforced at
`p.get`; a load asks for its class; the signing commands refuse an option they do not know), each witnessed red first. Row
**7.29** is new: a second `monitor_cli` test red under repeated runs, found reading RW 7.25's (each reading re-run against
its job log).

**This document is a list of gaps, not a list of defects.** Almost everything here is *already
named honestly somewhere in the repository* — that is the point of the project's honesty clauses,
and the reason this file could be assembled at all. What this file adds is a single place to see
the whole set, each item verified rather than inherited, with what closing it would actually take.

> **The one thing to understand before reading.** DeluluLang v1.0 is a working
> authority-and-effect-typed language whose central claim holds and has been attacked repeatedly.
> The gaps below are overwhelmingly **breadth** (a small standard library, one platform never run,
> a backend that compiles a subset) and **depth of evidence** (a paper proof rather than a
> mechanized one), not holes in the guarantee. Where a gap *is* in the guarantee — the microVM
> layer (built on Linux in 2026-09; still without its jailer), the same-uid boundary — it is called
> out as such and it is already documented.
>
> **2026-11-02: that note undersold the language itself.** It measured breadth by the standard library's size. Asked
> instead what a Java or Python programmer reaches for in an ordinary program, a probe found 25 of 39 everyday
> constructs refused (1.15): methods on one's own types, interfaces, `+=`, bitwise operators, ranges, string
> interpolation and search, a math library, tuples, sets, the command line (rows 2.33–2.40). With the slow default
> interpreter (3.24), those are what a new user meets first.

---

## 0. How to read the status column

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Not built** | The mechanism does not exist in the tree. Verified by reading the code, not by trusting a document. |
| **Partial** | Something ships and is narrower than the specification or the prose describes. |
| **Deferred (RFC)** | A ruling deferred it and named the reason; changing it needs the RFC process. |
| **Owner** | Reserved to the project lead. Not an engineering task. |
| **Environment** | Blocked by hardware or hosting this project does not have. No code closes it. |
| **Pending result** | Something has been triggered where this repository cannot observe it — CI on a remote — and no outcome has been read and recorded here yet. It is evidence in neither direction until it is. |
| **Closed** | Done since this document was written. Kept, struck through, with what closed it — this list is what a reader checks against, and a row that silently disappears reads as a row that was never there. |
| **Category 7** | Outside the proof boundary by construction (`MATHEMATICS.md` §12). Cannot be closed by code. |

Every row's *Verified* column says how I checked it in this pass. Where I reproduced a defect, the
measurement is given.

---

## 1. Corrections found while checking — claims that are stale, in both directions

These are not remaining work; they are **documents that disagree with the code today**. They are
listed first because a reader who trusts them would mis-plan. All are fixed: **1.11**, in an entrenched file, was the
project lead's to make until his delegation of 2026-10-17, and was made that day (7.10a, D-V2-141).

Two categories are deliberately **left alone**, and both are conventions this project already
holds. **Historical campaign records** — `CHANGELOG.md`, `HARDENING_CAMPAIGN.md`,
`STAGE10_BUILD_ORDER.md`, `docs/archive/v1/maintenance/` — carry the pre-fix statements as dated records of
what was true when written; this project does not rewrite its own history. And **the `dist/`
archive** (`dist/delulu-1.0.0-.../README.md`) is a frozen snapshot of what the 1.0.0 release
actually shipped, including its copy of the four-layer sentence. Editing it would misrepresent the
artifact. Release documents are exempt from the freshness scan for exactly this reason.

| # | Where | Said | Actually | Fixed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | `docs/QUESTIONS.md` §1.6 | *"the parser has no depth bound … Not fixed"* | All four recursive-descent classes are bounded at 128 — `DL0210`/`DL0211`/`DL0212`/`DL0213`. Reproduced: 50,000-deep expression → `DL0210`, **exit 1**, in contract. | ✅ |
| 1.2 | `docs/QUESTIONS.md` Part 5 item 14 | *"an unbounded parser recursion that crashes outside the exit-code contract"* | Same as above. The same file already said so correctly at §1.7 — it **contradicted itself**. | ✅ |
| 1.3 | `docs/design/AUTHORITY_GUARD_CAPSTONE.md` §2 surface 1 | Parser *"**Guarded, with one open crash**"*, *"no recursion bound … (P16, open)"* | Closed 2026-08-04/09. This is a current-state audit table, not a historical log, so it had to move. | ✅ |
| 1.4 | `docs/for-agents.md` `[agents.effects]` | Seven effect kinds | **Ten.** `Load`, `Async` and `Actuate` were missing from the one page an agent harness is told to pin. | ✅ |
| 1.5 | `HANDOFF.md` §6 | *"a fixed core set of **eleven**"* | **Ten.** `User` is the user-declared variant, not a fixed core effect. `CORE_EFFECT_NAMES` and the capstone both say ten. | ✅ |
| 1.6 | `crates/delulu-syntax/src/token.rs` | `ref`/`box`/`trn` are *"genuinely unbuilt"* | **All six reference capabilities work.** Verified by checking `iso`/`val`/`ref`/`box`/`trn`/`tag` as type prefixes — all six compile clean. They are reserved as *identifiers*; that is a different claim. | ✅ |
| 1.7 | `docs/design/LOCALIZATION_PLUGIN_GUIDE.md` §1 | delulu-slang *"Ships 100% of CLI strings"* | **Zero** when this row was written: `CLI_STRINGS` was an empty array — see 6.1. Three strings are registered since 2026-10-18, and the slang catalog speaks all three. | ✅ |
| 1.8 | `docs/lang/delulu-slang.md` | `coverage = { cli = 100, diagnostics = 90, explain = 25 }`, version 1.0.0 | Shipped catalog is **version 0.8.0, 8 of 157 diagnostics, 0 CLI strings**. | ✅ |
| 1.9 | `README.md` **Honesty section** | *"strength comes from defense in depth (type-system proof → WASM/WASI floor → **microVM containment** → human-held broker keys)"* | **Three of those four layers exist.** An unbuilt layer named as a source of strength, in the section whose entire subject is not overstating things. Also in `QUESTIONS.md` §1.3 and §2.1, and the Book Ch. 14, 15 and 20. | ✅ |
| 1.10 | `docs/archive/v1/design/LANGUAGE_SPECIFICATION.md` | *"This is the definitive design specification"* | It is the **superseded pre-implementation draft**. `REPOSITORY_STRUCTURE.md` had said so for some time; the document itself did not, so anyone opening it directly was told the opposite. Now carries its own status header naming the four decisions that did not survive contact. | ✅ |
| 1.11 | `docs/design/CONSTITUTION.md` §5.15 | Guarantee 5 names the microVM layer | Same overstatement — **but the file is ENTRENCHED** and was reserved to the project lead, so it was recorded as 7.10a and not edited. **Corrected 2026-10-17 (D-V2-141)** under his delegation: guarantee 5 names the layer for each way a program runs, the microVM only on Linux x86-64 with KVM (`ENTRENCHED_CHANGE_RECORD.md`). This row still said "Not edited" until 2026-10-22. | ✅ |
| 1.12 | `crates/delulu-check/src/check.rs`, the comment on `higher_order_callback_arg` | a unit test, `every_higher_order_callback_position_is_where_method_sig_expects_a_function`, *"pins each entry against `method_sig`"* | **No such test ever existed**, and the `Secret.map` entry was pinned by nothing (a mutant removing it passed every test). Now pinned by `secret_oracle.rs::an_impure_mappers_row_surfaces_in_its_caller`; the comment names the real pins (routine run 4, 2026-09-29). | ✅ |
| 1.13 | This file, rows 2.2 and 2.4 | `is_higher_order_method` names a `List.filter` that does not exist; 16 KB of nested list literals take 17.8 s to check | `List.filter` exists since P3; the parser refuses nesting past 128 levels (DL0210), and 16 KB at depth 120 checks in 0.39 s on a debug build (routine run 4). | ✅ |
| 1.14 | `docs/design/LOCALIZATION_PLUGIN_GUIDE.md` §2 | *"The full key list is enumerated in `docs/for-agents.md` / the Stage-9 reference"* | **Neither lists a catalog key**: `for-agents.md` names no `cli.*` key and no catalog, and no "Stage-9 reference" exists. The key space is `delulu_diag::REGISTRY` and `CLI_STRINGS`; `docs/lang/en-US.md` §3 gives every CLI string and a starter set of codes (7 of 154). Found 2026-10-18 (on-demand routine run 1) while finishing RW 6.1. | ✅ |
| 1.15 | This file — its opening note and row 2.1 — and `README.md`'s *Specified, not built* | The gaps are *"overwhelmingly **breadth** (a small standard library …)"*; 2.1, *"The standard library is very small"*, **Closed** by P3; the README: the one a new reader *"would otherwise meet the hard way"* is the optimizer and native backend | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): 39 small programs, each one construct a Java or Python programmer reaches for — **14 ran, 25 were refused**, and one of the 14 printed `hello {name}` with its braces. The owner, that day: *"Still cannot write complete program like in java or python, Most keywords and their equivalent are missing."* Deliberate and kept: no exceptions (Constitution §5.8, `Result` and `?`), no module-level `var` (DL0305), no implicit `Int` to `Str` in `+`, `let` for a constant, and faults that stop a program. The rest are rows 2.33–2.40. DeluluLang has 24 keywords; Python has 35. | ✅ 2026-11-02: rows 2.33–2.40 added; the opening note and the README row corrected |

Historical campaign records (`CHANGELOG.md`, `HARDENING_CAMPAIGN.md`, `STAGE10_BUILD_ORDER.md`,
`docs/archive/v1/maintenance/`) also contain the pre-fix parser statements. Those are **dated records of what
was true when written** and are deliberately left alone — this project does not rewrite its own
history. Only current-state documents were corrected.

## 2. The language and the compiler

| # | Item | Status | Verified | What closing it takes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | ~~**The standard library is very small.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-09-20 by V2 phase P3** (ruling D-V2-29). `List` has fifteen methods (`len`, `is_empty`, `get`, `push`, `pop`, `map`, `filter`, `find`, `fold`, `sort`, `reverse`, `concat`, `slice`, `contains`, `join`), `Str` has ten (gaining `to_upper`, `to_lower`, `replace`, `chars`), and **`Map[K, V]` exists** with eight (`len`, `is_empty`, `get`, `contains_key`, `insert`, `remove`, `keys`, `values`), constructed by the prelude builtin `Map()`. The higher-order additions carry their callback's row under R-4. | **Closed** | 23 new anchors, each with a POSITIVE and a NEGATIVE conformance witness (`tests/conformance/accept/29_stdlib_p3.delulu`, `30_map_p3.delulu`, and 23 reject files) — coverage is 353/353, 100%. `crates/delulu/tests/stdlib_p3.rs` holds the claims a conformance program cannot state: that `chars()` and `split("")` agree, that `keys()`/`values()` share one ascending order, that `find` short-circuits observably, that only the mutating methods demand a writable receiver (asserted in BOTH directions), and that each higher-order builtin surfaces its callback's row. The core-invariance snapshot gained new cases with **0 CHANGED and 0 GONE** — no existing program's answer moved. | **The row's old claim that "each method needs … a WASM lowering" is corrected, not satisfied:** none of these lower, because the backend has no `List` in its `Ty` at all and never did — `xs.len()` has always answered `DL1201` there. That was a rule the four pre-existing methods already broke, and a rule the code does not follow is not a rule (D-V2-29). **What is refused, each naming its reason:** `sort` on `List[Float]` (no total order — NaN compares false against itself); `sort` on any other unordered element type; `contains` on an opaque element, with the SAME code `==` uses (DL0605), because the runtime's structural equality answers `false` for two secrets and an admitted `contains` would return a confident wrong answer derived from secret data; a `Map` key that is not `Str`/`Int`/`Bool`. **Two defects OLDER than this row were found closing it** — MAP-PARAM-1 (`List.map` and `Secret.map` never checked their callback's parameter type, so a type error escaped to run time) and ARITY-LABEL-1 (`actuator`, `sensor` and `compute` had never been subject to the arity gate the primitive table calls normative). Both fixed, both witnessed, both closed as a class. |
| 2.2 | ~~**`is_higher_order_method` names `List.filter`, which does not exist.**~~ Fail-safe (it can never be called), but it is a hand-maintained list that has drifted from the table that defines it — design rule 1 in `STAGE10_BUILD_ORDER.md` §D14(d). | **Closed (2026-09-29, routine run 4) — overtaken by P3, and checking it found the pin it relied on missing.** `List.filter` exists since P3 (`prim_table.rs:149`, D-V2-29); the list is now `higher_order_callback_arg` (`check.rs`), carrying the callback's POSITION. Its doc comment named a unit test that never existed, and nothing pinned its `Secret.map` entry: mutant M10 (the entry removed) passed every test, DL0603 refusing the same programs for another reason. Now `secret_oracle.rs::an_impure_mappers_row_surfaces_in_its_caller` pins it (M10 red: no DL0501), `stdlib_p3.rs::every_higher_order_builtin_surfaces_its_callbacks_row` pins the four `List` entries (M8, `fold` read at 0, and M9, `filter` dropped, red), and the comment names both | `check.rs::higher_order_callback_arg`. Recorded in `HARDENING_CAMPAIGN.md` §"Findings recorded and NOT fixed". | A method added to the list needs its line in one of the two tests (the comment says so). |
| 2.3 | ~~**Type inference is exponential on a small class of programs.** No fuel bound, no `--max-type-size`, no timeout. `delulu check` is the agent hot loop, so this is a real denial-of-service surface.~~ Re-measured by the laptop 2026-10-23, release: `let aN = Pair { l: aN-1, r: aN-1 }`, 22 lines 5.8 s, doubling per line. The type is N small types through its variables; the checker wrote it out as its tree in the DIR's side table and at every field read, and followed the bindings as a tree in the occurs check, in unification (`a == b` of two such chains: 261 s at 22) and in `==`'s equality walk. | **Closed (D-V2-179)**: the occurs check and unification follow each binding once (no change in what they decide); the equality walk writes a type out once; and a type is written out within 100,000 parts, the module's large ones within 2,000,000 in all, past which the program is refused with DL0211 at the expression (language-visible: such a program checked before). Every depth refused in about 0.1 s, release; ordinary programs about 3% slower to check (10,000 functions: 1.141 s against 1.105 s). | `delulu-check/tests/type_size.rs`, four tests, each program checked within a minute: depths 16, 30 and 60 refused once, at `a16`, and 15 clean; a field read at 40; two chains compared, under the limit clean, past it refused, and `1` against `"x"` still a mismatch; 200 references to a 32,767-part type refused by the total, 40 to an 8,191-part type clean. Mutants red: no limit (4 of 4), no pair cache, an occurs check that re-follows, no DL0211 (4 of 4), the equality walk writing out each part again, the settle's replay naming no node | — |
| 2.4 | **Type checking is quadratic in nesting depth.** 16 KB of nested list literals took 17.8 s when measured; `fmt --check` on the same input is linear, so it is the checker. | **Partial — bounded (measured 2026-09-29, routine run 4).** The parser now refuses nesting past 128 levels (DL0210, the 2026-08 nesting-class fix), so no program reaches the old shape: 16 KB at depth 120 (64 nests) checks in 0.39 s, and at a fixed depth time is linear in size (depth 120: 64 → 256 nests, 0.39 → 1.63 s) — debug build, an upper bound. Per nest the cost still grows about as depth^1.6 (256 nests: depth 32 0.19 s, 64 0.60 s, 120 1.63 s). **Tried and refuted:** typing a list literal by its first element instead of a fresh variable (`check_list`) made it ten times slower (depth 120 × 256: 17.0 s) — reverted. | `HARDENING_CAMPAIGN.md` P16 findings list. | Profiling work; not a language change, so no RFC. |
| 2.5 | **No principal types.** Inference is order-dependent — swapping two parameters can decide whether a program compiles. Fail-closed, so no authority escapes. | **Open (F4)** | `SOUNDNESS_AUDIT.md` F-4; `CHECKPOINT-1.0.md` §8.16. | A real type-system change; RFC and probably a rewrite of `unify.rs`'s ordering. |
| 2.6 | **`Secret.verify` declassifies without requiring `Cap[Declassify]`**, while `expose` requires it. It *declares* the effect (so it is visible in the authority report) but needs no capability. Holding a secret therefore grants one chosen bit per call. Since `DELULU_CORE.md` v0.4 (2026-10-22, D-V2-175) the calculus says so too: `T-Verify` carries `Declassify`, as the implementation has since P17-IF1, and Fact B and Theorem 3 name `verify`'s label as the one no capability witnesses. | **Open (R-5 residue)** | `SOUNDNESS_AUDIT.md` F-5 box; witness `crates/delulu-check/tests/secret_oracle.rs`. Blocked concretely: closing it means `verify` returning `Secret[Bool]`, and `SecretVal` is **String-only** (`value.rs:620`). | RFC. Needs a non-String `SecretVal` representation first. **Under a lease** (V2 P5d, 2026-09-27, VERIFY-FABRICATED-1) the broker computes `verify` only when the lease holds `Declassify` and the Guard allows each secret; a local run is unchanged, so the row stands. |
| 2.7 | **No implicit-flow tracking.** This is not noninterference, and is not claimed to be. | **Category 7** | `MATHEMATICS.md` §12 "outside the boundary" item 1. | A different type system. Named as out of scope, not as a to-do. |
| 2.8 | **Resumable algebraic effect handlers.** Constitution §5.8 rejects exceptions and continuations for v1.0 and says handlers *"may be revisited post-1.0 only with a design that preserves the security reading of rows."* | **Deferred (RFC)** | No `handler` production in `parser.rs`. | RFC with a soundness argument — non-local control flow is exactly what effect rows are protecting. |
| 2.9 | **Reserved keywords with nothing behind them:** `async`, `await` (rejected by Constitution decision 12 — `Async` is an effect, not surface syntax), `trait`, `impl`, `where` (no typeclass design exists), `pure` (redundant with `!{}`), plus `plugin`, `secret`, `cap`. | **Deferred / rejected** | `token.rs:260` `RESERVED`. | `trait`/`impl`/`where` need a typeclass design and an RFC. The rest are decisions, not gaps. |
| 2.10 | ~~**Record field access is O(record width)** at run time (finding C55). Measured, U-shaped, minimum near width 20.~~ Re-measured by the laptop 2026-10-23 with a release build, best of three: 200,000 reads of the last of 1,600 fields took 0.473 s against 0.116 s for 4 fields, and a write the same. **Worse, and not named until then:** building a literal whose fields are written in another order than the type declares was quadratic in the width (RECORD-ORDER-1's sort, 2026-10-20, scanned the declaration for both fields' places at every comparison): 250 literals of 1,600 fields took 9.79 s, one of 6,400 fields 1.1 s, CPU that a plugin's fuel (one step per expression) does not count. | **Closed (D-V2-178)**: each record type's fields map by name to their declared place (`Home.records`), looked up once per field to put a literal in order; a record wider than 16 fields finds a field at its place and uses it only when the name there is the one asked for, else scans. Release, 1,600 fields: reads 0.128 s, writes 0.110 s, 250 literals out of order 0.078 s; 4 fields unchanged. Not through the DIR, as this row had planned: no format change, and no index trusted that a value from another module, a plugin or an actor did not follow. The checker was measured too: linear, 6,400 fields checked in 0.18 s. | `delulu-runtime/tests/record_width.rs`: the out-of-order literal against a list of its width (3,662x on the old interpreter, 17x now, bound 100), the last field of 8,000 against the first (21.4x on the old, 1.00 now, bound 3), a 40-field record held, read and written at its places, and a record whose fields are not where the declaration puts them read by name. Mutants red: no name check, a scan at every width, no ordering of a literal | — |
| 2.11 | ~~**No program can send a secret to an endpoint.** `Secret[T]` is opaque and the guest holds no secret bytes, but the language has no request that carries one: `http` has one method, `get(url)`, with no headers. For a value the order was reversed, so a capitalized function passed as a value was a constructor with no fields, *"not callable"*. A `let` or `var` nothing read checked with no word: the Book's `05_secrets` sample computed `ok` and printed *match* whatever it was, and a robotics demo built a command it never sent. | **Closed 2026-11-02** (D-V2-206) | `a_binding_never_read_warns_dl0414` (7 shapes) and `a_binding_read_is_not_reported` (9); mutants M1031–M1034, M1036 and M1037 red, M1035 equivalent; `core_invariance` changed by one warning in three reject programs | DL0414, a warning, names a `let` or `var` with no read when its block closes. An assignment does not read its target; the base of a field or element written does. A name beginning with `_` is exempt. |
| 2.31 | ~~**The editor's rename of a type or an effect left most of its uses behind**~~ (RENAME-TYPES-1, found 2026-11-02 by the laptop, sweeping every walk of the syntax tree after INDEX-TARGET-1). `name_occurrences`, which rename and find-references share, looked for a type or an effect in function signatures and constants alone. Renaming `Shape` changed 2 of its 9 uses: those in another type's fields, an actor's field, constructor and behaviour, a `let` annotation and a closure's signature stayed, and the renamed program did not check. A row in a behaviour, a closure or a test kept the old effect. | **Closed 2026-11-02** | `lsp_cli` `rename_finds_a_type_and_an_effect_wherever_one_is_written` (applies the edits and checks the program; red before); mutants M1038–M1043, each red | The walk visits every signature, field, variant, `let` annotation and closure signature, and every row an actor, a closure or a test writes (`walk_module`, `Visit::LetType`). |
| 2.32 | ~~**DL0414 named a binding never read and offered no edit**~~ (D-V2-206 left the repair out; the laptop drafted one on 2026-11-02, `3543faa`, and stopped before its suites ran). The draft's repair, a rename to `_x`, said it changed nothing the program does, and that was false wherever `_x` already meant something in the binding's scope (RENAME-CAPTURE-1): `let _x = 5`, `let x = 1`, then `_x` printed `value: 1` after the repair where it printed `value: 5`, and checked clean. In the editor the action carried no edit at all: the server dropped the edits of every `requires_human` repair. | **Closed 2026-11-02** (D-V2-207, on-demand routine run 17) | `delulu-check` `the_repair_is_withheld_where_the_new_name_is_already_spoken_for` (9 withheld, 3 offered); `fix_cli` `prefix_underscore_changes_nothing_a_program_prints` (runs each program before and after the edits); `lsp_cli` `a_repair_a_human_decides_offers_its_edits_and_is_never_preferred`; each red before its fix; mutants M1044–M1055 red | `prefix-underscore` (`requires_human`) renames the binding and each assignment, and is offered only when no `_x` is met between the binding and the end of its block (`NameClock`). A human-decided repair with edits is offered in the editor, never preferred. |
| 2.33 | **No methods on a program's own types, and no interfaces.** A function cannot be called as `p.area()` on a record or sum type the program declares; only the built-in types have methods. Nothing says that two types share an operation: there is no `class`, `interface`, `trait` or `impl` (the last two are reserved, row 2.9). Polymorphism today is a sum type and `match`, or a function passed as a value. | **Not built** | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `fn norm1(p: Point) -> Int`, then `p.norm1()` → DL0405 *type `Point` has no method `norm1`*; `class Point { … }` and `interface Shape { … }` → DL0208 *expected an item*. | Methods first (an `impl`-style block, or `f(p)` callable as `p.f()`), each method's row and reference capabilities checked as a function's are; then a typeclass design and an RFC for interfaces (2.9). |
| 2.34 | ~~**No compound assignment.**~~ `x += 2`, `-=`, `*=` and the rest did not parse; every update was written `x = x + 2`. | **Closed 2026-11-08** (D-V2-221, on-demand routine run 21, finishing the laptop's work in progress `2015f28`) | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `x += 2` → DL0202. Witnessed again on `18ea4e4` (DL0202 and DL0207) and `3` now. The work in progress read and wrote two places whenever the right side moved a name the target reads, witnessed on its binary: `ys[xs[0]] += bump(xs)` read `ys[0]` and wrote `ys[5]`. Tests: `compound_assign_cli` (both engines, the moved place, the refusal, the long form's codes and faults, `fmt`), the syntax crate's lexer, parser and fmt tests, the conformance accept file and two DL0207 rejects, `programs_cli`; mutants M1173–M1185 red. | The parser rewrites `t op= e` into `t = t op e` (the read with fresh ids; `Stmt::Assign::compound` for the formatter and the engines), and both engines find the place before the right side runs. A target holding a call is refused in words (DL0207, *bind the index first*), not run once: D-V2-221's choice. |
| 2.35 | **No bitwise or shift operators.** `&`, `|`, `^`, `~`, `<<` and `>>` on `Int` do not exist. | **Not built** | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `6 & 3` → DL0101 *unexpected character `&` (did you mean `&&`?)*; `1 << 4` → DL0202. | Six operators on `Int`, a shift past 63 bits a defined fault, on both engines. `|` is already a token (sum types, patterns). |
| 2.36 | **No range syntax.** A counting loop is `for i in range(0, n)`; `0..n` and an inclusive form do not parse. | **Not built** | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `for i in 0..3` → DL0201 *expected a member name, found `.`*. **Re-run 2026-11-05** (Claude's cloud review, a release build of `0670e0b` on Linux): `range(0, n)` itself runs on the interpreter and is DL1201 on WASM (row 3.25), so the documented spelling has one engine. Re-run again by the AI-first pipeline audit the same day, on the provenance build: unchanged. | A `..` token and a range expression, or a decision that `range` is the one spelling. |
| 2.37 | **Strings lack interpolation, indexing, search and formatting.** `"hello {name}"` prints its braces; `s[0]` is refused; there is no `find` or `index_of`, no `ends_with`, `repeat` or padding, and no number formatting. Joining a number needs `str(n)`, which is deliberate (no implicit conversion) and kept. | **Not built** | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `"hello {name}"` printed `hello {name}`; `s[0]` → DL0405 *cannot index a value of type `Str`*; `s.index_of("l")` → DL0405. **Number formatting** (the laptop and on-demand routine run 22, 2026-11-08/09, found by RW 2.38's programs): `str` of a `Float` prints every digit of a large or tiny magnitude, so `str(100.0.exp())` is a 44-digit number and `str(1.0e300)` has 301 digits, where Python prints `2.6881171418161356e+43`. | Interpolation checked as `str()` is (a secret refused, DL0604 as now), `Str` indexing by character with a defined fault past the end, and the missing methods in the primitive table, each with conformance witnesses. |
| 2.38 | ~~**No math library, and no way to ask before a fault.**~~ No `abs`, `min`, `max`, `pow`, `sqrt`, `floor`, `ceil`, `round` or trigonometry. Integer division by zero and overflow stop the program (DL0902, DL0901), which is deliberate, but there is no checked form to ask first. | **Closed 2026-11-09** (D-V2-223, on-demand routine run 22, finishing the laptop's `ee207ce`) | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `abs(-3)` → DL0301 *unknown name `abs`*; `sqrt(2.0)` → DL0301; `10 / z` with `z = 0` → DL0902; `9223372036854775807 + 1` → DL0901. **Closed by:** 33 pure methods on both engines — `Int`'s `abs`, `min`, `max`, `clamp`, `pow` and five `checked_` forms (`None` exactly where the operator faults, a law `math_parity` checks on both engines over a grid); `Float`'s `abs` … `log10`, `is_nan`, `is_finite` and the checked conversion `to_int`. The transcendental functions are `libm`'s, the same digits on every OS. `abs(x)` is DL0301 saying `x.abs()`. Tests: `math_tests` (7), `math_parity` (5), `math_spelling_tests` (3), conformance (`accept/31_math.delulu`, five rejects, 399 of 399 anchors), programs `math_table`, `hypotenuse`, `loan` and `weather` (303 lines) on both engines, checked against Python; mutants M1201–M1219 and M1206b red. Its programs found FMT-MATCH-ARM-1 and FMT-FLOAT-EXP-1 (both fixed), the untyped WASM compile's missing math import (fixed), and 2.48 and 2.49. | Methods rather than prelude functions, because a prelude name is refused as a declaration (C11) and programs declare `fn abs` and `fn max` (D-V2-223). |
| 2.39 | **Collections lack tuples, sets, keyed sorting and comprehensions.** No `(1, "a")`, no `Set`, no `sort_by` or comparator (`sort` uses the natural order), no `[x * 2 for x in xs]`. | **Not built** | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `(1, "a")` → DL0201 *expected `)`, found `,`*; `Set()` → DL0301; `xs.sort_by(…)` → DL0405; a comprehension → DL0201. | Tuple types and patterns; `Set[T]` beside `Map` (ordered, for the same deterministic iteration); `sort_by` carrying its callback's row (R-4). Comprehensions may stay `map` and `filter`. |
| 2.40 | **A program cannot read its command line or its environment, set its exit code, or sleep.** `Root` mints none of these; an environment variable can be read only as a secret (`root.secret(NAME)`). | **Not built** | Everyday-program probe, 2026-11-02 (the laptop, `delulu run --grant console`, a debug build of `master` `9c51b4e`): `root.args()`, `root.env("HOME")`, `root.exit(3)` and `root.sleep(10)` → each DL0405 *type `Root` has no method …*. | Each a capability with its grant, named in the authority report, `--within` and the sandbox channel, so the program still does only what it was handed. An exit code may be `main` returning a `Result`. |
| 2.41 | **No JSON, and no way to write a correct JSON string.** Neither the prelude nor the standard library parses or writes JSON, and `Str`'s table has no escape, so a program builds JSON by concatenation, and a value holding `"` or a line break makes it invalid. | **Not built** | Claude's cloud review, 2026-11-04 (a release build of `master` `0670e0b` on Linux x86-64; `docs/review/DeluluLang_Claude_Independent_Strategic_Verification.md` §6.2): `parse_json(s)` → DL0301 *unknown name*; `prim.rs`'s `Str` table (`len`, `trim`, `contains`, `starts_with`, `split`, `to_upper`, `replace`, `chars` …) holds no escape. Codex's review found the same gap (its §1). | `json.parse` and `json.write` in the pure standard library, with byte, depth and number limits, a secret refused as `str` refuses one, and their conformance anchors. Library-only, so no core RFC (`CONTRIBUTING.md` §5); the anchors touch the entrenched `delulu-conform`. |
| 2.42 | **A scope refusal cannot be handled.** In `match wr.write_text("../x", …) { Ok(_) => …, Err(e) => … }` the write outside the grant is DL0904 and stops the program: the `Err` arm never runs, so a program cannot fall back to a path it holds. | **Open — a decision** | Claude's cloud review, 2026-11-04 (a release build of `master` `0670e0b` on Linux x86-64; the review's §6.2). | Decide it deliberately: a refusal stays a fault (today, the Constitution's fault rules), or becomes an `Err` the program may handle, with the authority report unchanged either way. Changing the fault rules is a semantic change: the owner's word or a recorded `D-V2-nn` (the review's §13, owner decision 3). |
| 2.43 | **Two authoring traps a newcomer or a model meets first are taught nowhere.** `else` on the line after `}` is DL0202 *"expected an expression, found `else`"*; a parameter named `cap` is DL0106 (a reserved word). | **Open — teach** | Claude's cloud review, 2026-11-05 (a release build of `master` `0670e0b` on Linux x86-64; the review's §6.2): each cost a check cycle while its simulations were written. `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md`'s table of first errors names neither. | Both in the first-program material (`GETTING_STARTED.md`, `docs/for-agents.md`, `delulu new`'s template), or a hint on DL0202 when the token after a line break is `else`. |
| 2.45 | **A record held in a list or an `Option` cannot be written through its binding.** `for a in accounts { a.balance += 1 }` and `xs[0].bal += 5` are DL1604 *"cannot write a field through a `val` receiver"*; `Some(a) => { a.bal += 5 }` is DL1604 *"the receiver's reference capability could not be determined — annotate it"*; passing the element to a `ref` parameter is DL1603. Each holds with the list typed `List[ref Acc]` and the option `Option[ref Acc]`. A record bound directly (`let a = Acc { … }`) is written freely, so an everyday program keeps its changing numbers outside its records (the ledger program keeps its balances in maps). Found too by the laptop, 2026-11-06: a list in a record's field cannot be written in place, `p.ys[0] = …` is DL1604. | **Open** (a basics gap; a decision) | On-demand routine run 21, 2026-11-08, writing `crates/delulu/tests/programs/ledger.delulu` on a debug build of `95610bd`: six probes, each refused as quoted. A loop's binding takes the element type's default capability, read from the checked `List[T]`, which carries none (`rcap_check.rs`, `Stmt::For`). | Carry a container's declared element capability to the binding a loop, an index or a pattern gives (a `ref` list of `ref` records yields `ref` records), or decide and teach the one spelling that writes. Either is a decision about the reference-capability rules, taken with the soundness audit's laws (`iso` and `val` stay contained), never a relaxation by default. |
| 2.46 | ~~**A `let` binding can be reassigned, and DL0207's explanation says it cannot.**~~ `let x = 1`, then `x = x + 1` (or `x += 1`), checked clean and printed `2`. The parser recorded `mutable` on every binding, and no pass read it. `delulu explain E-DL0207` said *"a `let` binding cannot be assigned — `let` is a binding, not a variable"*. | **Closed 2026-11-08** (D-V2-222, the laptop) | Measured first: of the repository's 267 `.delulu` files none reassigns a `let`, except 29 in `measurements/ai-usability/runs/`, which models wrote (`c1__t1`'s and `c2__t1`'s final programs among them; the pilot is re-scored). The witness: on `459e410`, `let total = 0` then `total += v` printed `sum: 12`; now DL0207, and after `delulu fix` it prints `sum: 12`. Tests: `delulu-check` `let_binding_tests` (3), `fix_cli` `let_to_var_migrates_a_program_that_reassigned_a_let`, conformance `accept/var_and_let` and three DL0207 rejects; mutants M1186–M1194 (M1194 survives: an unreachable fallback, which refuses). | Only a `var` is assigned. A `let`, a parameter, a `for` loop's variable and a pattern's binding are DL0207, naming which (`check.rs` `Binder`, `lookup_to_assign`). A `let` carries the exact repair `let-to-var`, which `delulu fix` applies. A field or an index written through any binding is the reference capability's business, as before. DL0207's explanation is corrected to say so. The Agent Skill teaches it. `STABILITY.md` §5's *"a diagnostic made to fire where the spec always said it should"*, called out in the CHANGELOG. |
| 2.47 | ~~**A name bound by a pattern to plain data could not be stored in a record's field.**~~ `Some(v) => { p.x = v }`, with `v` an `Int`, was DL1603 *"cannot store an undetermined capability where `val` is required"*, while `p.x += v` passed: the reference-capability pass gave every pattern-bound name no capability. | **Closed 2026-11-08** (with D-V2-222, the laptop) | Found by `crates/delulu/tests/programs/gradebook.delulu` (`st.best = v`). Neighbours probed: `Some` of a literal and of `parse_int`, through a `let`, `Ok` and `Err`, a program's own variant, nested; a loop's variable and `v + 0` were already clean. Tests: `delulu-check` `pattern_binding_rcap_tests` (2, the twins included); mutants M1195–M1200, all red. | `rcap_check.rs` `bind_pattern` takes the scrutinee's checked type and passes each variant field's type down (`Some`, `Ok`, `Err`, a program's own sums). A name bound to a value with no write surface (`has_no_write_surface`: plain data and the handles) is `val`. A record or a list out of a pattern stays undetermined. Its type's default may be `val` while the value is a `ref`: a `ref` list out of `Some` stored in a `val` field is still DL1603. That is RW 2.45's decision. |
| 2.48 | **A Float literal written without a fraction is refused with errors that do not say so.** `1e300` and `1e-9` lex as the `Int` `1` followed by the name `e300`: the grammar reads an exponent only after a fraction (§2.1, `1.0e300`). So the program gets DL0201 *expected `)`, found identifier `e300`* or DL0301 *unknown name `e300`*, never the spelling it needs. Most languages accept `1e300`, so a model writes it. | **Not built** | On-demand routine run 22, 2026-11-09, `79431e4`'s binary: `[0.0, 1e300]` → DL0201 *expected `]`, found identifier `e300`*, DL0301 *unknown name `e300`* and a DL0401 cascade. | The lexer names the spelling: an `Int` literal immediately followed by `e` or `E` and digits is one malformed literal, DL0104, *"a Float literal needs a fraction: write `1.0e300`"*. Accepting `1e300` instead is a grammar change, and takes its own decision. |
| 2.49 | **A line that begins with a binary operator does not continue the expression, even inside parentheses.** `(a` then `+ b)` on the next line is refused; `(a +` then `b)` checks. A long formula broken before its operator, as many style guides prefer, is refused, though inside a parenthesis nothing can end a statement. | **Not built** | The laptop, 2026-11-08 (RW 2.38's work in progress), re-run by on-demand routine run 22 on `79431e4`'s binary: DL0201 *expected `)`, found end of statement*, DL0202 *expected an expression, found `+`*, DL0209. | Inside `(`…`)`, `[`…`]` and a call's arguments, a newline does not end a statement, as in Python and Go; or the refusal says to move the operator to the end of the line. |
| 2.44 | **`check` holds about 14 KiB of memory per source line.** 224 MiB and 580 ms for a generated 16,002-line program; 315 MiB to compile it to WASM. One `delulu lsp` process on a 4,002-line file plateaued at 76.7 MiB. | **Open — measured** | Claude's cloud review, 2026-11-04/05 (a release build of `master` `0670e0b` on Linux x86-64; the review's §10.2 and §7): generated programs of *n* chained functions, peak RSS read at each size. | Find where the memory goes (the tree, the checker's tables, the diagnostics) before changing anything. It matters for edge targets and for long editor sessions on large files. |

---

## 3. Backends and execution

| # | Item | Status | Verified | What closing it takes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | **The DIR-level optimizer described in spec §2.1 does not exist** — no cross-package inlining, no monomorphization, no escape analysis. | **Not built (RFC-gated)** | `AUTHORITY_GUARD_CAPSTONE.md` §2 surface 3: *"none is implemented"*; `STAGE10_BUILD_ORDER.md:2060`. | A real optimizer plus evidence the passes pay off. Deferred under ruling **D18c** partly because no such evidence exists yet. |
| 3.2 | **There is no native backend.** `@jit` exists only as a *leash*: without `--grant exec.native` the hint is ignored with **DL1906**, and `exec_native: false` is hard-coded on the lease path so a federation delegation can never confer it. | **Not built (deliberately leashed)** | `run_cmd.rs:375`, `cli.rs:2553`, `codes.rs:1444`. The CLI itself says *"no native tier exists in v1.x"*. | A native tier plus the invariant-45 obligation that every mode passes the same suites. Constitution §5.11 also requires that only human-controlled policy may grant native emission. |
| 3.3 | **The tiered execution model of Constitution §5.11 does not exist.** No profiling, no on-stack replacement, no deoptimization. `@aot`/`@interpret`/`@jit` parse and are reported; nothing switches tiers. | **Not built** | Same as 3.2; `attributes_cli.rs` tests only that the hints are *recorded*. **Measured by the AI-first pipeline audit, 2026-11-05 (a release build of `master` `0670e0b` with its commit injected, on Linux x86-64; `docs/review/DeluluLang_AI_First_Compiler_Execution_Pipeline_Audit.md` §10):** the engine is chosen by hand and each WASM run compiles with Cranelift again. That costs 1.6 ms of a 1.8 ms hello run and 652 ms of a 16,002-line program's 676 ms; a precompiled module loads in 35 µs and 5.7 ms. Neither engine wins everywhere: a 5M-iteration loop took 910 ms on the interpreter and 34 ms on WASM, a 4,002-line program doing little work 136 ms against 179 ms for its `.dwx`. Winch compiled 15–33% faster but ran the loop 43% slower, and Wasmtime 48 documents epoch interruption, which the limits engine uses, as incompatible with it. Pulley ran it 11× slower than Cranelift. | Follows 3.2. |
| 3.4 | **The WASM backend compiles a subset of the language.** Five of six benchmark kernels hit **DL1201** and fall back to the interpreter — unbounded loops in `main`, string building, and the macro workloads are outside it. **Stage 10 acceptance criterion 1 is NOT MET** and is published as such. **Widened 2026-10-19 (the laptop, D-V2-163):** `while`, `break`, `continue`, assignment to a `var`, `Str.len()`, `str(Bool)` in any program and a memory that grows compile — 4 of the 6 kernels run on WASM with the interpreter's output. **Widened again 2026-10-20 (the laptop, D-V2-164):** a `List` of scalars compiles (a literal, `len`, `is_empty`, `get`, `push`, indexing, `for`, `map`) and `Str.split`, so all **6 of the 6 kernels** run on WASM with the interpreter's answers; a list of lists stayed DL1201 until 2026-10-22 — the other ten `List` methods compile since 2026-10-21 (D-V2-171: `pop`, `reverse`, `concat`, `slice`, `contains`, `filter`, `find` and `fold` in the guest, `sort` and `join` host-side, and every walk that runs code given a private copy of the slots in a module that pops, which is why `pop` had waited; `list_methods_parity.rs`, 7; `contains` on records and sums since 2026-10-22, by the host's `eq`) — (`Str == Str` and `!=` compile since the same day, and so do the other eight `Str` methods — `trim`, `to_upper`, `to_lower`, `chars`, `contains`, `starts_with`, `replace`, `slice` — each answered host-side by the interpreter's own `call_str_method`). A function named as a value compiles too (`apply(double, 21)`), and a generic function's body brings what it needs (2026-10-20). `parse_int` compiles, answered by the interpreter's own `call_builtin`; so does `Float` — arithmetic, comparison, negation, `%` (the host's), `float`, `int`, `str` and `parse_float` (2026-10-20). An empty list with no written type takes the one the checker inferred (`compile_checked`, which the CLI uses; 2026-10-20). Records compile — a literal (its fields in the order written), a field read and write, sharing as the interpreter's (2026-10-20); so does a field holding an `Option`, a `Result` or a `List` of scalars — a linked list (`next: Option[ref Node]`), a tree (`kids: ref List[ref Tree]`) — and a constructor with no written type (`let c = Red`, `var cur = Some(head)`, `var o = None` typed by the checker; 2026-10-20). `str` and `==`/`!=` of a list, an option, a result, a sum or a record compile too — answered host-side by the interpreter's own `Value::display` and `Value::eq` over the value rebuilt from the guest's memory by its type's descriptor (`reify.rs`), so a value that holds itself, a `NaN` and a `Float`'s text are decided once for both engines (2026-10-20; with the checker's types — `compile_checked` — which say where the host's value interface is needed). `Map` compiles (2026-10-21, D-V2-170): `Map()`, all eight methods, a key of `Int`, `Bool` or `Str` and any value a list may hold, `str` and `==` of a map. It lives in the guest's memory, and the host finds and places each key by the interpreter's own `MapKey` order (`delulu:map`), so both engines iterate it identically (`map_parity.rs`, 4; M718–M733 red but M725, which no test can see). Since 2026-10-22 these nest (D-V2-173): a list of lists, a list of options, a map of lists, an option of an option, a map inside a list, an option or a record field — a slot holds a pointer to any list, map, option or result, as it holds a record's (`nested_parity.rs`, 4). A list or a record field holding a function stays DL1201; one holding a capability or a secret compiles since 2026-10-23 (D-V2-177): a slot holds the handle's index into the host's table, as a variable does, in a list, an option, a record field or a map's value (`handle_parity.rs`, 2). The corpus's entry points: 11 of 17 compile (2026-10-20, `04_effects`, `03_errors`, `telemetry_report`, `02_option_and_lists` and `01_types` the sixth to tenth, the tier-5 `attenuation` the eleventh — `Cap[FsRead].narrow` minted host-side by the interpreter's own `resolve_in_scope`); `root.fs_write`, `write_text` and `append_text` compile too (2026-10-20), performed host-side in the interpreter's order — the record, the custody gate on the path the write will open, `resolve_in_scope`, the write through `beneath` — with the interpreter's trace byte for byte; and `root.http` with `Cap[Http].get` (2026-10-21), the interpreter's own mint and `get` run host-side in its order — the record, the custody on the URL's host, its egress client — **12 of 17**, all six guide chapters; the rest stop at Python, a plugin, a secret in the guest (`demo`) or a package's other modules. **A package builds whole since 2026-10-22:** `build <dir> --target wasm` compiles the program `run <dir>` runs — resolved, checked as a workspace, flattened — to one `.dwx` with its manifest's scopes (`target/<name>/<name>.dwx` by default), so the two counted as stopping at their other modules, `tier4-multimodule/station` (four packages, seven modules) and `examples/greeter`, build and run as programs — **14 of 17**, re-measured file by file the same day (the 12, `demo` DL1205, the plugin host and `numpy_mean` DL1201, the two packages' entry files DL0301 alone); `package_run.rs` `a_four_package_program_builds_to_one_dwx_that_runs_as_the_package_does`. **A secret since 2026-10-22 (D-V2-174):** a WASM program holds a `Secret[Str]` as a handle the host keeps: `root.secret`, `get_with_secret` (sent from the host by the interpreter's own code, with the audience, the custody and the broker's `send` record as there) and `verify` compile; `expose`, `root.declassify()` and `Secret.map` stay DL1205. So `demo` runs on WASM: **15 of 17**, re-measured file by file the same day (the plugin host and `numpy_mean` DL1201 remain); `wasm_cli` `demo_runs_on_wasm_as_on_the_interpreter_and_its_secret_stays_on_the_host`, `egress_cli` `a_wasm_program_sends_its_secret_from_the_host_as_the_interpreter_does`, `host::secret_handle_tests`. | **Partial — widened (RW 3.4)** | `measurements/study-c/HOT_PATH_TABLE.md` — **re-measured 2026-10-19 18:46 UTC over all six** (release, every lane but Go, the laptop's WSL): WASM runs **6 of 6** at 9.8×–15.7× C whole-process (geo-mean 12.4×), the interpreter 8.9×–489.5× C; the earlier runs kept in `runs/2026-07-20/` and `runs/2026-10-19-vm/` (the cloud VM's, 4 of 6 at 6.5×–11.5×). `STAGE10_BUILD_ORDER.md` D18b. `delulu-wasm/tests/loop_parity.rs`; `differential.rs` with loop programs (50,000, 0 mismatches); `budget_cli.rs`'s two WASM budget witnesses; `list_parity.rs` (9) and the loop generator's lists (2026-10-20); `str_parity.rs` (3) and the console generator's `Str` methods (each method in over 100 of `conformance_parity`'s 2,000 programs, asserted; the 50,000-program differential, 0 mismatches) | A compute-only ratio: the start-up control exists (0.55 ms C, 3.58 interpreter, 6.24 WASM in the published run), but C's work above it is 0.04–0.14 ms, inside its start-up's noise, so the kernels must run long enough for C's work to be measured. Then the speed itself: the DIR-level optimizer (3.1), and `split`'s host crossing, which leaves `wordcount_macro` slower on WASM than on the interpreter. |

The 50,000-program gate generated no function value, so it could not see it. | **Closed**: a block opens a frame of function values beside its locals. | `loop_parity.rs` `a_function_value_bound_in_a_block_is_that_blocks` (red on the old backend: `2 23 33 300` against `7 21 31 301`); the loop generator binds, calls and shadows `h` (149 of `random_loop_programs_agree_with_the_interpreter`'s 400, asserted; red on the old backend); the 50,000-program differential with it, release: 46,235 agreed, 3,765 both faulted, 0 mismatches; mutants M510–M512 red | — |

Other matches compile to the same bytes. | `delulu-wasm/tests/match_parity.rs` (4) — red on `30885b1` (`red,red,red,none` for `red,other Green,blue,none`); mutants M756–M766 red | — |

| 3.24 | **The interpreter is slow on loops and calls** (found 2026-10-24 by the agent test campaign's builder, Sonnet 5.5, A14): a merge sort of 100,000 `Int`s took 28 s on the interpreter and 56 ms on `--engine wasm`. A variable lived in a `HashMap<String, Value>` per scope, so each read hashed its name in every scope it passed and each assignment allocated the name again. Every block and every `match` arm made a scope, `let` or not. A call made a vector of its arguments and two scopes, and every `Some` and every scalar `==` allocated. On Windows one allocation and its free cost about 55 ns. | **Narrowed 2026-10-29; again 2026-11-01 (on-demand routine run 16, profile first)** | `step_allocations` (4 tests): a counting loop allocates nothing per step, a call allocates its scope and its names' room, `at0(xs, i)` 7, and the bindings stay where they were (the counting loop's test was red before: `==` allocated); `value::scope_tests` (3); mutants M870–M880. Measured on the laptop with the old binary and the new, the same output: the merge sort 8.2 s → 5.0 s, quicksort 3.8 s → 2.5 s, Study C's `loop_sum_1m` 694 → 327 ms, `nested_calls_macro` 527 → 287 ms, `fib_recursive_24` 160 → 101 ms. **2026-11-01 (on-demand routine run 16, D-V2-202):** `step_allocations` `a_call_of_five_parameters_makes_its_room_once` and `a_block_of_five_lets_makes_its_room_once` (3.00 allocations a step before, 2 after); `value::scope_tests` `a_shared_filter_bit_hides_nothing`, `the_nearest_scope_answers_first_at_every_size` and `a_cleared_scope_finds_nothing_and_binds_afresh`; mutants M994–M1003, each red. Measured in the VM with the harness `scripts/interp-profile.sh` keeps (interleaved, the minimum of three rounds, against `a4da001`): `loop_sum_1m` 157.0 → 147.5 ms, `fib_recursive_24` 42.2 → 38.5, `nested_calls_macro` 144.4 → 117.1, the micro set 324.3 → 275.4, the merge sort and quicksort of 100,000 3,913 → 3,526 ms; in instructions −12.3%, −13.1%, −15.9%, −13.4% and, on the sort of 20,000, −12.4% (7,451 → 6,529 million). | The interpreter is still a tree-walker that finds each variable by name, about 100× slower than WASM on computation. **Measured 2026-11-01 (the laptop, release binary of `cccb001`, 2,000,000 iterations each):** a bare `while` loop 155 ns an iteration (about ten nodes, ~15 ns a node, every name in the first scope); a call to a module function +105 ns, a closure +140 ns, an index +50 ns, two nested blocks with a `let` each +370 ns; the WASM engine ran every one in under 60 ms with startup. **Tried and refuted** (each built, timed three times, reverted): boxing a fault in `Escape` (an `R<Value>` about half its size) and keeping a scope's first four bindings inline (one allocation fewer per call and per binding block) each changed nothing beyond noise, so neither the size of what an evaluation returns nor the allocation is the cost. The next attempt starts from a profile, not a guess. Closing it takes variables resolved to slots before the run (a resolver that mirrors every scope the interpreter makes), or a compiled form. Until then, `docs/for-agents.md` says which engine to choose. **The profile (callgrind, on-demand routine run 16, `a4da001`):** a step of `loop_sum_1m` is 2,069 instructions — `eval_expr`'s own body (its dispatch and its 56-byte result) about 390 of them, `binary` about 300, the block's statements about 300, the drop glue of the step's temporary `Value`s 168, a variable's read about 150 and an assignment about 140; a call of `nested_calls_macro` about 1,800 (SipHash twice, a scan of the prelude's names, a miss walk of every scope, two allocations); the sorts spend a fifth of theirs in `malloc` and `free`. Run 16 cut what a lookup and a call could shed (the evidence cell) and refuted two more cuts by their counts: a scope's first four bindings held inline (+2–5% on every benchmark — the laptop's timing confirmed) and an inlined clone of scalars (+3%). What is left is the tree-walker's own shape — the dispatch, the result, the drop glue, the walk — so the next step is the one already named: variables resolved to slots before the run, or a compiled form. Start from `scripts/interp-profile.sh --profile NAME`. |
| 3.5 | **A multi-threaded WASM engine** — Wasmtime running the actor scheduler across OS threads with shared-everything-GC. | **Deferred, with published note** | `docs/design/THREADED_WASM_DEFERRAL.md`. Nothing is lost today: multi-threaded actors already ship TSAN-clean **on the interpreter**; the WASM scheduler is cooperative single-threaded by design. | Re-opens when the wasm shared-everything-GC + threads proposals mature in a pinned Wasmtime. Then port the scheduler and **re-run Stage-7 criteria 1, 6, 7 on the new engine**. |
| 3.6 | **Bare-metal / MCU targets** — compiling DeluluLang itself for microcontrollers. | **Deferred (RFC)** | `STAGE10_SPECIFICATION.md:346`. | RFC. Not a claimed capability anywhere. |
| 3.25 | **WASM refuses three constructs the documents teach.** Each is DL1201 at compile time, so nothing runs, while the interpreter runs the program: `for i in range(0, n)`, row 2.36's documented counting loop (*"a call to `range` (not a compilable function)"*); a **non-generic** function with a function-typed parameter, `fn apply(f: fn(Int) -> Int, x: Int)`, given a named function or a lambda (`codegen.rs`'s `is_compilable` asks a `wasm_ty` of every parameter, and a function type has none); and an `if` without `else` used as a match arm's value (`Some(p) => if p > 2 { found = true },`). The generic `fn apply[T, U](f: fn(T) -> U, x: T) -> U` compiles (`named_fn_parity.rs`), and the arm wrapped in a block, `{ if … }`, compiles. Row 3.4 names none of the three. | **Open** | Claude's cloud review, 2026-11-04/05 (the review's §6.2), each re-run 2026-11-05 on a release build of `master` `0670e0b` on Linux x86-64 before this row was written: the three refused on `--engine wasm` and answered on the interpreter; the generic and the block forms print the same on both engines. Codex's review reported `range` (its §1). | Lower the three, or refuse them before execution and name the spelling that compiles (`check --target wasm`, row 6.61); a parity test for each, held to the interpreter's answer. |
| 3.26 | **Every `delulu` invocation pays 1.1–2.0 ms to load `libpython`, used or not.** The default build links embedded CPython at load time, so the process floor of the commonest agent commands rises by a third or more: `delulu --version` took a median 3.35 ms against 2.21 ms for the same source built without Python, and `check` of a two-line file 3.65 ms against 2.39 ms (31 runs each; `/bin/true` 0.90 ms). With both builds stripped, two more rounds of 40 runs gave 3.72 against 2.30 ms and 4.84 against 2.86 ms. The front end itself took 85 µs. **Re-measured 2026-11-05 by the hidden-complexity audit** (100 interleaved runs of each build, its §5.1): 1.35 ms a command (`--version` 4.54 against 3.19 ms, `check` 5.17 against 3.83 ms in its harness), and ~~0.4 MiB (`--version`) to 4.5 MiB (`check`)~~ **4.1–4.3 MiB on every command** more peak memory. On `--engine wasm` they stop with DL0904 *"expected 0 arguments, got 1"* (`fn main(root: Root) -> Int`: *"expected 1 results, got 0"*), Wasmtime's words at run time rather than DL1201 at compile time, and `build --target wasm` exits 0 with no diagnostic, writing an artifact whose every run is that fault after *"authority verified"*. | **Closed** | `crates/delulu/tests/wasm_cli.rs` `every_main_shape_runs_on_wasm_and_as_a_dwx_as_on_the_interpreter` — seven shapes (`fn main()`, `-> Int`, `-> Str`, `-> Bool` by a string comparison, a list, `(root: Root) -> Int`, `(root: Root)`), each run on the interpreter, on `--engine wasm` and as a built `.dwx`, the three alike — red on the old code (`no_root on --engine wasm: error[DL0904]: expected 0 arguments, got 1`) and green now; the unit test `a_main_the_host_cannot_call_is_refused_at_its_name` (codegen.rs, the unchecked path). | 3.29 | ~~**On a host that caps address space, a deep recursion aborts the process instead of DL0905.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-11-06 by the laptop (D-V2-216).** The CLI runs on the largest rung of `INTERPRETER_STACK_LADDER` (about 781 MiB, 256, 64, 16, 8 MiB) the host grants, the main thread when it grants none (1 MiB assumed), and records that stack before any work starts (`delulu_runtime::on_laddered_thread`); every interpreter built without a bound takes the one that fits it (`process_max_depth`). A sandbox guest also lowers that stack to half the memory ceiling the OS enforces on it (`ceiling::own_memory_ceiling`: a Windows Job Object's per-process or per-job limit, Linux's `RLIMIT_DATA`), because a Job Object counts the stack pages a recursion commits however large the reservation. A bound the host lowered says so in the fault: *"recursion depth exceeded (the bound is N here: …)"*. Actor workers already had their own ladder (D67); a sandbox guest refuses actor programs. Was: `main.rs` (lines 82–102) runs the CLI on a thread reserving `INTERPRETER_STACK_BYTES`, 10,000 × 80 KiB + 256 KiB, about 781 MiB (`crates/delulu-runtime/src/interp.rs` lines 33, 56, 90), so that the depth bound fires before the stack runs out; when the reservation fails it falls back to the 8 MiB main stack. Under `ulimit -v 300000`, `--version`, `check` and `run` still worked, but a recursion 100,000 deep, which is DL0905 with a report without the cap, ended with *"thread 'main' has overflowed its stack"*, exit 134, and no `--report-out` report. ~~`--sandbox` and `--limits` are not affected: they limit `RLIMIT_DATA`.~~ **Corrected 2026-11-05 (second pass, the audit's §11.2), and worse:** `--limits mem=268435456` without the sandbox still gives DL0905, but the sandbox's own memory ceiling triggers the abort. Under `--sandbox --sandbox-profile hostile-agent` (256 MiB), the profile `docs/for-agents.md` recommends for code you did not write, or `--sandbox --limits mem` up to 768 MiB, the guest prints *"has overflowed its stack"*. The host reports `{exit 1, ran: true}` with no diagnostic. DL0905 returns from 896 MiB, and on `contained` (1 GiB). Without the sandbox, the abort appears under any `ulimit -v` below about 940,000 KiB. WASM under a 2,000,000 KiB cap refuses cleanly (DL0904 *"mmap failed to reserve 0x104000000 bytes"*, report written). | **Closed** | `crates/delulu/tests/sandbox_run_cli.rs` `a_deep_recursion_under_the_hostile_agent_ceiling_is_dl0905_not_an_overflow` (not macOS, which refuses `hostile-agent`, D-V2-59) — red on the old code on Windows (*"guest: thread 'delulu-main' has overflowed its stack"*, the laptop) and on Linux (`witness.yml` run `42225562026`, ubuntu-24.04, branch `laptop/stack-ladder-witness`: *"guest: thread 'main' has overflowed its stack"*), green with the fix on the laptop; `crates/delulu/tests/cli_contract.rs` `under_a_capped_address_space_deep_recursion_is_dl0905_with_a_report` (Linux, `ulimit -v 600000`, the report asserted) — red on the old code (run `42225556840`: *"thread 'main' has overflowed its stack"*); the unit test `the_ladder_runs_on_the_largest_stack_granted_and_records_it` (interp.rs, its own recorder). ## 4. Containment and security

> This section is the one where a gap can touch the guarantee. Read 4.1 and 4.4 in particular.

| # | Item | Status | Verified | What closing it takes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | **The microVM isolation layer — built on Linux x86_64 with KVM (V2 PS-C, 2026-09-27).** Until then `crates/delulu/src/microvm.rs` was a 58-line *probe* whose `probe()` returned `Err` on every path, including the fully-provisioned one. It is now the launcher: the interpreter runs as PID 1 of its own kernel under Firecracker, with no network device, no filesystem device and one vsock channel to the host, which performs every effect with the code that serves an L1 guest. The design changed with D-NE-23: no virtio-fs scope mounts and no in-guest egress proxy — the guest has no filesystem device and no IP stack at all, and the host decides each request. The image (a kernel with vsock and nothing else, built from pinned kernel.org source, and an initramfs holding the static guest) is checked against its manifest on the copy that boots. | **Built (Linux x86_64); partial** | `run --isolation microvm` runs a program at level 2 and reports it. `tests/microvm_cli.rs` (the lifecycle family: T8, T10, T11, T13, the probe) and Stage 5 **criterion 8, restated for the no-NIC guest** (`microvm_criterion8.rs`, T9) pass on a KVM host, each falsified by a mutant; gated on `cfg(delulu_kvm)`, and the `microvm` CI job builds the image and runs them. Where it cannot be given, DL1408 as before — nothing weaker ever launches under the `microvm` name. `V2_PS_C_PREREQUISITES.md`, D-V2-39. | Not yet: **the jailer for an ordinary user** — run as root, the VMM runs under Firecracker's jailer (a uid of its own, a chroot, a reaper in place of the death signal; D-V2-40), but the jailer needs root, so an ordinary user's L2 run still names `identity_separation` as a limitation; **cgroups and a network namespace for the VMM** (D-V2-40 §5); **aarch64**; **Cloud Hypervisor**; and a **distributed image** (PS-C-05, owner-reserved: D-NE-27, distributing a built GPL kernel). Constitution §5.15 guarantee 5 (*"violations are contained at the WASM/microVM layer"*, entrenched) now has its microVM half on Linux; its wording stays the owner's. |
| 4.11 | ~~**A program cannot load a plugin at run time.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-09-20 by V2 phase P2** (ruling D-V2-27). `root.plugin_host()` mints `Cap[PluginHost]` under `--grant plugin=<path-or-dir>`; `load(host, path, grant)` resolves the path inside the granted roots through the same containment `fs.*` uses, checks the package's `[plugins] allow` hash ceiling against the bytes in hand, and runs `load_verified` steps 1–6 — the same functions `plugin verify` calls, so the two cannot disagree. `p.get` returns a callable, `p.unload` revokes, and the per-call liveness re-check (R-6c) kills a retained callable. | **Closed** | `crates/delulu/tests/plugin_load_cli.rs` — 11 cases, each paired with the control that shows the same program succeeding once the refusal's cause is removed, and four of the gates falsified by mutant. `examples/plugin_shout/host.delulu` runs under `examples_run.rs` and prints the plugin's own answer. A Verified plugin executes its **DIR**, which `STAGE6_SPECIFICATION.md` §3.2 already calls canonical (`delulu:wasm` is a cache recompiled from DIR when invalid). | **What is still refused, each with its reason in the refusal:** non-zero `Grant.limits` (the interpreter has no fuel meter, so a limit nothing enforces is refused rather than ignored); a grant carrying `Declassify` or `ForeignCall` (custody-enforced, and a plugin's export runs in its own interpreter which cannot share the host's); the `Contained` class on Windows, unchanged. The first two are PS-B/PS-C work. The `DL0703` complaint in the old row is answered: the code now means what it says, because there IS a grant to name. |
| 4.2 | **Per-plugin microVMs.** Plugins share the host's profile in v1.0. | **Not built** | `STAGE6_SPECIFICATION.md:298`, `STAGE6_PLUGINS_GUIDE.md:56`. | Follows 4.1. |
| 4.3 | **A real seccomp filter** for foreign workers. The shipped isolation is the process-separated worker; seccomp *"needs a heavy dep and is deferred."* | **Deferred** | `STAGE5_SPECIFICATION.md:653`. | A dependency decision plus Linux-only code. |
| 4.4 | **The same-OS-user boundary cannot be closed by code.** *(Narrowed for one case by PS-B-03, 2026-09-25: a `run --sandbox` GUEST on Windows now runs as a per-run AppContainer with no capabilities — no network, none of the operator's files, not the state directory, measured with a control (T14). Narrowed again by PS-B-03b the same day: a Linux guest, where the host allows user namespaces, runs as a subordinate uid (4.21). The broker, the CLI, every unsandboxed run, and guests on macOS or on a Linux host that forbids user namespaces are unchanged.)* To the kernel, a process running as your user *is* you: it can read `broker.key`, edit `root_policy.json`, or kill the broker. | **Category 7** | `ROOT_ISSUANCE_TRUST_BOUNDARY.md` §1–§2; verified with a real second UID in `docs/security/red-team-p21-crossaccount-2026-08-08/`. | **Nothing. This is a deployment decision, not an engineering task** — run untrusted code as a separate OS account (Tier 2 of `DEPLOYMENT.md`). Naming it as remaining *work* would be the mistake. |
| 4.5 | **Strict anchored-root mode (the DISC-1 mitigation) is opt-in.** Legacy is still the default, so a same-user process can mint root authority unless the operator turns the gate on. | **Opt-in in 2.0 (D-V2-148)** — the flip planned for a later major with ROOT_ISSUANCE_TRUST_BOUNDARY §10's migration | `broker start --require-anchored-roots` works end to end (fixed 2026-08-10); `doctor` reports the mode; the banner reports all three states. Default flip is analysed in `ROOT_ISSUANCE_TRUST_BOUNDARY.md` §10. | A **major** version, a loud migration note, an `--allow-unsigned-roots` escape, and the auto-root re-pointed at "adopt a pre-issued root". §10(E) lists the compatibility shims that would *weaken* the boundary and must be refused. |
| 4.6 | ~~**CONTAIN-TOCTOU-1.** Filesystem containment is check-then-open, so a *concurrent* writer into a granted directory can swap a checked file for a symlink in between.~~ **Closed 2026-09-27 by FS-RACE-1 (V2 P5c)** — recorded here on 2026-09-28, while writing the cloud handoff: this row had gone on saying "accepted" for a day after the fix. | **Closed** | `prim.rs`; documented in `DEPLOYMENT.md` §5. The confined program cannot win this race through the primitive table — no symlink-creating operation exists. | Closing it properly needs `O_NOFOLLOW`/`openat2` — the platform-dependent containment this project deliberately refuses. Deployment rule instead: grant scopes only to directories the program's own user can write. |
| 4.7 | **A signed Verified-class hardware adapter — BUILT (V2 P8-02, routine run 14, 2026-10-05, D-V2-96/D-V2-97).** `delulu run --broker-profile hw:NAME --adapter-dpx FILE --adapter-transport CMD --adapter-signer HEX`: the driver's logic is a Verified `.dpx`, read once, its embedded signature verified against the pinned key, its DIR replayed by the checker, and interpreted from those same bytes — so nothing is started by name and the check-then-start residual below does not exist for it. The plugin holds no authority and its four exports are pure; the host's transport is the only part that touches the machine. `examples/line_driver`, `crates/delulu/tests/hw_dpx_cli.rs`, `crates/delulu-runtime/src/verified_adapter.rs`. The row's history: **A signed Verified-class hardware adapter.** What ships is an operator-supplied **subprocess** over a stdio line protocol, not specification §5.4's signed plugin. Its provenance **is** checked before it is spawned (D52/D53: a detached signature, refused when bad, optionally required and pinned; D66: recorded) — this row said "no signature check on the adapter itself" until 2026-09-28, stale since D52. What is missing is the Verified-class form: a `.dpx` re-proved at load, dispatched only after its signature verifies against a pinned key. | **Built** (the `--adapter-cmd` subprocess form remains, unchanged, for a driver that is not a plugin) | `CROSS_PLATFORM_VERIFICATION.md` §"first hardware adapter"; `AUTHORITY_GUARD_CAPSTONE.md` §2 surface 8. **2026-09-28, ADAPTER-SPELL-1 (the first cloud routine run):** the check read `--adapter-cmd`'s first word from the working directory and the spawn let the OS look it up on `PATH` — witnessed: a pinned check passed on `./drive` and an unsigned `drive` from `PATH` ran. Fixed: resolved once (`cli::resolve_driver`), and that file is verified and started; `hw_adapter_cli.rs` `a_bare_driver_name_is_verified_and_started_as_one_file`. Residual: a replacement between check and start by someone who can write the driver's directory. **2026-10-05, routine run 13 — V2 P8-02 begun:** its build order read against the code (`V2_P8_DESIGN.md`: a Verified `.dpx` already runs by interpreting its re-proved DIR, and DL1504 is its purity rule) and the broker's driver slot made an `adapter::Adapter` trait (`418dcca`); a control program also runs in a sandboxed guest now (P8-01, D-V2-95). | The §5.4 signed-plugin path. Named in `CHECKPOINT-1.0.md` §9.6 as what a real device deployment would require. |
| 4.8 | **The audit anchor is not tamper-proof.** It catches truncation and naive tampering; an attacker who rewrites the log *and* `ANCHOR.json` is not caught. | **Named residual** | `MATHEMATICS.md` §12 item 2; pinned by a **passing** test. | An external witness — a second party or an append-only service. Not a code fix inside this repository. |
| 4.9 | **Multi-tenancy is not provided.** Every part of the model assumes one holder per host. Under a *shared* broker state directory a co-tenant can enumerate the grant tree and read `broker.key`. | **Not provided (by design)** | `AUTHORITY_GUARD_CAPSTONE.md` §3; `broker_transport.rs:5` confirms peer credentials check "same user", never multi-tenant auth. | Separate OS accounts or containers. Named as the answer, not as a gap to fill. |

The `ml-dsa`/`ml-kem` crates are, by their own authors' statement, never independently audited. | **Partial** | `measurements/pqc/KAT_RECORD.md`. **The NIST ACVP vectors were obtained and are saved** in `measurements/pqc/vectors/` across five test types. **4.10a done (V2 P7, 2026-09-27):** `crates/delulu-runtime/tests/nist_kat.rs` checks every saved case the pinned crates can express, byte-exact — ML-DSA-65 keyGen (5), sigGen (15; the HashML-DSA and randomized-over-μ groups, 3, are named and not checked — no API), sigVer (9, both verdicts), ML-KEM-768 keyGen (5), encaps/decaps/key checks (12) — and asserts each file's NIST provenance; a mutant (the wrong FIPS 204 domain separator) fails it. | Two independent gates from invariant 51 / ruling **D14b**: (a) wire the saved vectors into a byte-exact test — **done (4.10a, V2 P7)**; (b) an independent audit of the crates, which is not something this project can perform — **still open**, so PQC stays behind `--unstable`. |

| 4.14 | **The foreign-worker channel had no read deadline** (NE-21): a foreign library that never returned hung the host. | **Closed (PS-0-07)**; the deadline was per read, so a reply DRIPPED one byte at a time still held the host — closed 2026-09-30 (FRAME-DRIP-1, D-V2-73): the whole reply is owed within the call's deadline | `WorkerConn` now carries IPC-1's read deadline; a timeout is `WorkerDied` and the worker is killed. | — |
| 4.15 | **There was no resource bound on the main program, on either engine** (NE-22): the interpreter bounded call depth only, the main program's WASM store set no fuel, epoch or limiter, and an unbounded mailbox grew past 1 GB under `--grant console` alone. | **Closed (PS-B-01, 2026-09-25)** | Every ordinary run is budgeted — 1 GiB of memory and 5 minutes of processor time by default (owner ruling D-V2-25, answering D-NE-31), `--limits mem=,cpu=,wall=` to set others, zero refused — by a host watchdog sampling the process's peak memory and processor time every 25 ms (`crates/delulu/src/budget.rs`), so the interpreter, the WASM engine and every actor thread are held by one mechanism.
