| 4.16 | **A granted host NAME was not checked for the address it resolves to.** PS-0-09 refused special-use addresses and names under plain `--grant net=` at grant time; a public-looking name that resolved to one could only be caught at connect time. | **Closed (PS-B-02, 2026-09-25)** | The egress client resolves host-side, classifies EVERY candidate with `netclass::addr_class` (the grant-time tables, widened to the IANA special-purpose registries; NAT64 and 6to4 judged by the IPv4 address they carry), refuses unless the host was granted with `net.special=`, and hands the client only the classified addresses (`egress::tests::a_granted_name_that_resolves_into_a_special_range_is_refused`). | A LEASED run cannot reach a special-use range at all: the broker's node has no `net.special` dimension yet, so the lease carries none (fail closed; V2 log, PS-B). |
| 4.17 | **Two path questions PS-0 left open** (D-V2-24). ~~POSIX names that change under Unicode normalization are not refused (only an embedded NUL is): the tree has no normalization library.~~ **The normalization half decided and built (2026-10-18, D-V2-156):** measured on macOS — APFS treats NFC and NFD as one name, `realpath` gives an existing file one spelling, but a name not yet created kept its written spelling and a Guard seal written in the other was walked past (witnessed red); the Guard now compares path rules in NFC on macOS, and nowhere else (on ext4 and NTFS they are two files). On Windows an operator's `fs.*` grant may name a UNC path (a program's own paths may not); reaching `\\host\share` can send the user's NTLM credentials to that host, so such a grant is the operator's informed choice — **and since 2026-10-22 it is said:** on Windows a `fs.read`, `fs.write` or `plugin` grant naming a share prints, once, the server it reaches and that reaching it can send the user's NTLM credentials there; the run goes on (`cli.rs` `a_grant_naming_a_network_share_is_warned_of_on_windows`, three mutants red). | **Closed (2026-10-22)** — both halves | `prim.rs` path refusals (PS-0-06); `guard.rs` `path_within`/`one_normalization` and `a_path_rule_covers_the_other_unicode_normalization_where_the_filesystem_ignores_it` (red on macOS before the fix); `prim.rs` `two_normalizations_of_one_file_resolve_to_one_spelling` (the measurement) | — |
| 4.19 | ~~**A hardware driver's own start-up counts against its first exchange.**~~ **Closed 2026-10-17 (D-V2-147):** a driver (and a Verified driver's transport, and `delulu device sim`) prints `READY` first; the host waits for it before `main`, for `--adapter-startup-ms` (default 10 s, at most 120 s), and refuses the run on silence or any other first line. `EXCHANGE_TIMEOUT` is untouched: start-up no longer counts against the first exchange. | **Closed (2026-10-17)** | Witnessed by `adapter.rs`'s `a_slow_starting_driver_is_waited_for_and_its_first_command_answered` and `a_driver_that_does_not_say_ready_is_refused_before_any_command`, and `hw_adapter_cli.rs`'s `a_driver_that_never_says_ready_refuses_the_run_before_main_and_the_wait_is_the_operators`; five mutants killed (D-V2-147). Breaking for driver authors: CHANGELOG. | — |
| 4.20 | ~~**`run --sandbox` dropped most of `run`'s flags in silence.**~~ The sandboxed path read six of `run`'s ~45 options. `--lease` was never redeemed (the guest ran holding nothing, and the resulting DL0703 told a lease holder to "pass `--grant console`"), `--broker daemon` got embedded custody, and `--trace-out`, `--engine`, `--isolation microvm`, `--seed`, `--approved` and the rest were ignored. So were a misspelled flag, a flag with no value and a second file, all of which the ordinary path refuses (P1-F3): those refusals ran after the dispatch. | **Closed (found in PS-B-05, 2026-09-25)** | `guest.rs::refuse_what_the_guest_does_not_apply`: an ALLOWLIST of the flags a sandboxed run applies (`--sandbox`, `--sandbox-profile`, `--mode`, `--limits`, `--grant`, `--json`, `--report-out`, `--no-prompt`), with the ordinary path's own two refusal functions called first. `sandbox_modes_cli.rs::a_flag_the_sandboxed_run_does_not_apply_is_refused_not_dropped`, falsified by two mutants (no guard; no allowlist). Found by following the Survey's blast radius of the budget change into `guest.rs`. | Each refused flag is work for the channel (PS-C): broker custody and leases for guests above all. **Done 2026-09-27 (P5b):** `--lease`, `--broker daemon` and `--epoch-ms` apply to a sandboxed run — the host authorizes every guest request through the broker; `sandbox_guard_e2e.rs`. |
| 4.21 | ~~**A Linux sandbox guest still runs as the operator's uid.**~~ The roadmap's PS-B-03 asked for "uid mapping where namespaces allow". An unprivileged user namespace alone maps the guest to the operator's OWN uid outside, which separates nothing for files; a real second identity needs a subordinate uid, i.e. the setuid `newuidmap` helper, an `/etc/subuid` entry for the operator, and unprivileged user namespaces the host allows (Ubuntu 24.04 restricts them through AppArmor). | **CLOSED (PS-B-03b, 2026-09-25, D-V2-37) where the host allows it** | Measured first (`host-capability-probe` `linux-subordinate-uid`: Ubuntu's default forbids it; with its one sysctl lifted a subordinate-uid child was refused the operator's `0600` file the control read). Built: the guest is born as uid 1 in its own user namespace, mapped to a per-run subordinate id, with no groups or capabilities, its binary executed through a descriptor (no runtime copy) and its channel an inherited socket. T14 on Linux (`identity::linux::tests`, a control, `PUBLIC=true` for what every account may read) falsified twice (no uid change; groups kept). CI's x86-64 job requires it; arm64 exercises the fallback. | Where the host forbids user namespaces the guest is the operator, says so, and `doctor` gives the launcher's reason; the boundary there is a separate OS account (`DEPLOYMENT.md` Tier 2). Residual: other tools (rootless containers) share the subordinate ranges. |
| 4.22 | ~~**There was no break-glass, and nothing for one to break.**~~ D-V2-09's operator escape hatch existed only as a `doctor` line saying so. | **Closed (PS-B-06, 2026-09-25)** | `breakglass.rs` (D-V2-35): `sandbox require` makes the sandbox mandatory on a host; a signed, single-use, one-program ticket (at most a day) is the only way past it, audited or refused. `break_glass_cli.rs` walks the transition matrix; six mutants. | Under the policy `test` and `repl` are refused outright: they have no sandboxed form yet (PS-C) and take no ticket. A same-user process can delete the policy file (category 7, 4.4). |
| 4.23 | ~~**A Linux run report does not count what the guest applies to ITSELF.**~~ Landlock and the seccomp filter were applied by the guest and announced on its standard error, but never told to the HOST, so `posture` listed writes, network and new programs as not confined on a Linux run that confined all three. Seen reading a PS-B-03b run's report (2026-09-25). | **Closed (2026-09-25, same day)** | The guest now sends `Confined { applied }` as its FIRST request — after its lock-down, before the program's first line — and the host accepts it only first, once, and only in the words of `channel::SELF_APPLIED` (anything else refuses the whole report, and the guest then does not run). `delulu-sandbox-channel/2`. Unit test with two mutants killed (any word accepted; accepted after other requests); on Linux the posture test now requires `new_programs` to be counted, and a WSL run's report reads reads-confined, writes-denied, new-programs-denied with only `network` left (that kernel predates Landlock's TCP rules). | — |
| 4.18 | **Two PS-0-08 experiments are not yet conclusive** (run `35376590803`). The macOS Seatbelt network probe is void: without the sandbox its own curl timed out (exit 28), so the sandboxed failure (exit 7) proves nothing. On Windows a grandchild process ran inside a Job Object whose active-process limit is 1: either the limit does not hold or the probe assigns the job after the child starts. | **Closed (probe fixes `85501ec`, run `35378619727`)** | `.github/workflows/host-capability-probe.yml` | Seatbelt: wait for the server's port before probing. Windows: create suspended, check `AssignProcessToJobObject` returns true, then resume; re-run. **Result:** both were probe flaws. The Windows grandchild is blocked with 1816 (not enough quota); Seatbelt: plain exit 0, sandboxed exit 7. |
| 4.24 | ~~**The attestation seam (PS-D-02) is designed, not built.**~~ An external launcher (L3, PS-D-01) is reported as measured by nobody; nothing let its operator's attester vouch for it, or let a run refuse to serve a guest that is not attested. | **Closed (2026-09-28, the first cloud routine run, D-V2-48; `fef8ccd`, CI `36395256154` green on every job)** | `--require-attestation HEX` with `--sandbox-backend external:CMD`: a statement signed over this run's nonce by the pinned key is checked before the program is sent; the report's `sandbox.attestation` carries the attester's claims as the attester's; `delulu sandbox attest` is a software reference attester. `crates/delulu/src/attest.rs` (six unit tests) and `tests/sandbox_attest_cli.rs` (five end-to-end, the binary as attester, launcher and guest); six mutants falsified (`V2_LOG.md`). | Not integrated with hardware: level 4 `attested` waits for a hardware attester whose quote the host checks itself. No lease-level "only attested" constraint (RFC territory). |
| 4.25 | ~~**The sandbox boundary is confirmed by convention, not by construction, and a missing layer never refuses.**~~ `guest.rs` does check before it sends (RW 4.23's self-report, PS-D-02's attestation), but nothing in the types stops a path that sends first; and when a layer is absent — no Landlock, no second identity — the report says so and the run proceeds, for every profile including `hostile-agent`. Only an attested L3 run has a per-run generation. **Worse than "by convention", found building it (2026-09-28, routine run 2): the host's FIRST frame was the program, so a guest that never confined itself had already received it** (witnessed red on `ff701ae`). | **In progress — the order and the generation built (PS-E-01's first step, D-V2-56): `delulu-sandbox-channel/3`, `boundary.rs`'s typestate, a generation in every report and launch record; and the five properties reported by every run (D-V2-57); and `hostile-agent` requires all five, refused before the program is sent (D-V2-59). and (routine run 11, D-V2-87) at L3 a claim of the pinned attester that names a property meets it, the state staying `unknown`; and (routine run 12, D-V2-90) a macOS guest's memory ceiling is the host's sampler, so `resource_ceiling` holds on every OS. **Closed (2026-10-05, routine run 12, D-V2-92):** `contained` requires egress, resource and host-loss confinement and refuses, before the program is sent, a boundary this host measured without one; an external launcher's unknown wall is reported, not refused (only `hostile-agent` demands proof). Not in `contained`'s set: `filesystem_confinement` (macOS's reads, a residual of PS-E, D-V2-93)** | `guest.rs` launch sequence; `host_guarantees`, `fully_enforced`. Found in the study of NVIDIA OpenShell (2026-09-28), whose backend contract makes an unconfirmed launch unrepresentable. | `Launched → Confirmed → Running`; five properties each established/absent/unknown with mechanism and evidence; a generation for every run; a profile's required property that is absent refuses before the program is sent (`hostile-agent`: all five). `docs/DELULULANG_V2/V2_OPENSHELL_STUDY.md` §4.1. |
| 4.26 | **A macOS guest that computes without asking outlives its host** until its processor-time limit (five minutes by default), and **an external launcher is started with no death signal and no Job Object**. Linux (`PR_SET_PDEATHSIG`, guest and VMM) and Windows (kill-on-close) end the guest with its host. | **Closed (2026-09-29, routine run 3, D-V2-60)** — the Linux launcher's death signal (2026-09-28); the macOS watcher for the guest and the launcher (red `36526271163`/`36526351005`, green `36526707055`/`36526709263`/`36526711462`); the Windows launcher's kill-on-close job (red `37828ab`, `witness.yml` run `36527876892` — "the external launcher outlived its host by more than 3.0 s", green `1a63829`: `36528140459` (`sandbox_confirm_cli`, 7 passed — the launcher gone 20 ms after its host was killed), `36528149629` (`sandbox_external_cli`), `36528152057` (the jail unit tests), `36528154367` (`sandbox_run_cli`) — all success). What a launcher starts OUTSIDE itself stays the launcher's | `jail.rs`'s own note on macOS; `guest.rs::launch_external` (`Jail::none()`). | A guest-side watcher on macOS (`kqueue` `NOTE_EXIT`, `getppid` fallback); a death signal or Job Object for the launcher; per OS, a spinning guest gone within 1 s of the host's kill, measured. `docs/DELULULANG_V2/V2_OPENSHELL_STUDY.md` §4.2. |
| 4.27 | **Hypotheses — the Linux guest's kernel surface after lock-down (not yet witnessed).** H1: the seccomp filter does not name `io_uring_*`, `userfaultfd`, `memfd_create`, `pidfd_*`, the new mount API, `kexec_file_load`, or `clone` with `CLONE_NEWUSER`. H2: Landlock mediates TCP only, so an escaped guest could create UDP, netlink or Unix sockets — and, as the same uid, connect to the operator's own Unix sockets. H3: the Landlock rule grants reads beneath all of `/proc`, so a same-uid guest could read other processes' `environ`. H4: the host is dumpable during a sandboxed run. H5/H6: the Landlock ABI requirement; macOS `mach-lookup` and Windows named objects. | **In progress — H1, H2, H3 confirmed and closed, and H7 found and closed (2026-09-29, routine run 3, D-V2-61: GUEST-SYSCALL-1, GUEST-SOCKET-1, GUEST-PROC-1, GUEST-DEV-1 — `jail::escaped_tests`, six mutants red); H4 confirmed and closed the same run (HOST-DUMPABLE-1, D-V2-62); H8 found and closed (GUEST-TIOCSTI-1, D-V2-63 — an escaped guest typed into the operator's terminal); H9 found and closed (GUEST-SIGNAL-1, D-V2-64 — it could signal the operator's processes); H10 found and closed (GUEST-PROCESS-1, D-V2-65 — it could change their limits, priority, CPUs and scheduling); H5 confirmed and closed (LANDLOCK-TRUNCATE-1, D-V2-66 — a pre-ABI-3 kernel's report claimed writes denied); H6 open (routine run 5's start on it was stopped by a safety classifier before anything ran)** | `jail.rs::lock_down_self` (18 syscalls denied, + fork/vfork on x86-64) and `confine_filesystem` (`SYSTEM_READ` includes `/proc`); no `PR_SET_DUMPABLE` anywhere in the tree. Compared against OpenShell's documented Linux controls (2026-09-28). | An escaped-guest test mode; then deny `socket` after lock-down, narrow `/proc` to `/proc/self`, the host non-dumpable, the missing syscalls denied (`clone3` answered `ENOSYS`) — each witnessed, fixed and falsified, or refuted with its evidence. `docs/DELULULANG_V2/V2_OPENSHELL_STUDY.md` §4.3. |
| 4.28 | **The external launcher is looked up on `PATH` when it is started, and never hashed** — the shape ADAPTER-SPELL-1 had for hardware drivers. The report names the program, not its bytes; an attestation does not say which launcher ran. **Witnessed (2026-09-29, routine run 5): a relative `PATH` entry let a `lnch` planted in the working directory run instead (LAUNCHER-SPELL-1).** | **Built, partly (2026-09-29, routine run 5, D-V2-69)** — resolved once by `resolve_driver`, hashed (BLAKE3) from the opened file, `launcher_path`/`launcher_blake3` in the report and the launch record, `--launcher-digest` refusing any other file before it starts (recorded), and on Linux the descriptor hashed is the file started (`fexecve`); five witnesses, mutants M1–M6 red. **Windows built (2026-09-30, routine run 6, D-V2-74):** the file held open sharing reads only from the hash until the run ends — witnessed red on Windows first (a launcher renamed over while it was hashed was started). **The attestation binding built (2026-10-05, routine run 11, D-V2-89):** a `delulu-attestation-v2` statement carries `launcher_blake3`, measured by the attester (`sandbox attest --measure-launcher FILE`), and is refused unless the launcher started has that digest; M103–M107 red. **Open:** macOS has no `fexecve` | `launcher.rs`; `guest.rs::launch_external`, `serve_under`. | Resolve once in DeluluLang (`resolve_driver`'s code), hash the opened file, start that file; the digest in the report, the audit and the attestation statement; `--launcher-digest` to pin. `docs/DELULULANG_V2/V2_OPENSHELL_STUDY.md` §4.4. |
| 4.29 | ~~**No program can be checked against a boundary, and nothing asks the operator for authority.**~~ `authority --grants` prints what a program needs, and a delegation wider than its parent is refused (DL0802), but there is no "does this whole program stay within these grants?" answer with a counterexample, no risk report on a change of grants, and a refusal cannot become a request to the operator. | **Closed 2026-11-01** (the laptop, the weekly verification sweep's documents scan: the row's condition was met on 2026-10-13 and its status still read *"P9-04 and P9-05 next"*) — P9-01 built (routine run 20, D-V2-118: `authority FILE --within BOUNDARY`, four results, the counterexample's call, the coverage); P9-02 built (the same run: `grants diff` between grant files, D-V2-119, and lease nodes, D-V2-120); P9-03 read against the code, its step 1 built (D-V2-121: a refused run's report records the refusal at its call) and step 1b (routine run 21, D-V2-122: a sandboxed run's report records the host's refusal) and step 2 (the same run, D-V2-123: `grants propose REPORT`, the refused call's own requirement); both for a package directory (D-V2-124); step 3 (routine run 22, D-V2-125: `grants propose --node`, `proposals`, `approve` under the approver's own node, `reject` with a reason); step 4 (the same run, D-V2-126: `grants auto-approve --parent g_B`, the standing node approving what it bounds) — **P9-03 complete**. P9-04 (a secret bound to its endpoints) and P9-05 (method and path scopes) came after them, from the study's §4.11–§4.12, not this row's §4.8–§4.10 (`V2_PHASE_STATUS.md` row 16). Checked against the binary of `cccb001`: `authority --within`, `grants diff`, `grants propose`, `proposals`, `approve`, `reject` and `auto-approve`, each in `delulu help grants` or `authority --help`; `within_cli` (24 tests) and `broker_cli`** | `delulu authority`; `grants delegate`. Found in the study of NVIDIA OpenShell, whose prover checks a policy against a boundary and whose advisor turns denials into reviewed proposals. | `authority --within BOUNDARY` (four results, a source-located counterexample, the dimensions covered); `grants diff` (categorical findings); `grants propose`/`approve`/`reject`, auto-approval only within the operator's standing `⊑` boundary with no finding, effective on the next run. `docs/DELULULANG_V2/V2_OPENSHELL_STUDY.md` §4.8–§4.10. |
| 4.31 | ~~**An external (L3) guest's self-report counts as a host guarantee.**~~ The words a guest reports applying to itself (RW 4.23) enter `host_guarantees` and the posture at every level; at L3 the guest is a binary the operator's launcher chose, so a lying guest makes its report say "writes denied, network only the channel" while `sandbox.properties` (correctly) says `unknown` — the report contradicts itself. Found by the red-team pass on `/3` (2026-09-28, F7). | **Closed (2026-09-28, same run)** — witnessed red on `42f5ea0` by the head chef; the guest's words are now `sandbox.guest_reported` at L3, never host guarantees or posture; mutant M11 killed | `guest.rs::serve_under` (the self-applied words merged into `applied`); `policy.rs::posture` | At L3, report the guest's words as the guest's (`guest_reported`), never in `host_guarantees` or the posture, unless an attester's statement names them. |

| 4.30 | **NVIDIA OpenShell is neither a tested L3 backend nor a target DeluluLang can write policy for.** | **Closed (routine run 10, 2026-10-04): (a) the policy export (routine run 8, D-V2-82); (b) the guest inside OpenShell at L3 (D-V2-83, D-V2-85), read green on a runner, the recipe in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`** | PS-D-01 documents a Docker + gVisor recipe only. `delulu sandbox policy --format openshell` now writes the policy (never wider than the authority and the grants; checked by OpenShell's prover in `openshell.yml`, and ENFORCED by a real OpenShell sandbox in its `runtime` job — the granted program runs inside it, a narrower grant is refused DL0703, a file the export never names and `curl` to an unlisted host are refused by OpenShell, its `NET:OPEN … DENIED` line read; routine run 8). | 4.35 | ~~**The broker daemon's REPLY write has no bound**~~ **Closed 2026-10-04 (routine run 10, D-V2-86, REPLY-HOLD-1): the whole reply owed within 5 s, each write within 1 s — Linux and macOS by a socket write deadline, Windows by the pipe's own quota accounting (a first Windows design, `PIPE_NOWAIT`, failed on the runner, `37236818679`, and was withdrawn); witnessed red first, read green on every OS; the status cell said Open until 2026-10-19** (the red-team pass on FRAME-DRIP-1, F2): a client that asks for a large answer and never reads it holds the one-connection serve loop — the e-stop's revoke behind it. A `GuardRequest` needs no credential and takes a long `why`, so any same-user client can make a `GuardPending` answer large. **Re-run by the head chef on `68b8888`:** an `Inspect` answer of ~250 KB left unread held `broker status` past 8 s (~180 KB did not — the socket's buffer). | **Closed** (routine run 10, D-V2-86) | `brokerd.rs` (`serve_inner`'s `write_frame`); `broker_transport.rs` (Windows `WriteFile` blocks) | A whole-write deadline (a per-write timeout alone repeats FRAME-DRIP-1: a reader taking a byte every few seconds keeps it alive); caps on a request's field lengths and on `List`/`GuardPending` answers. |
| 4.36 | ~~**`delulu-registry serve` has no bound on a connection** (F3)~~ — **closed 2026-09-30 (routine run 6, D-V2-76, REGISTRY-BOUNDS-1)**: one thread, `incoming()` in turn, `read_line` with no deadline or cap, and `vec![0; Content-Length]` before reading. **Re-run by the head chef:** one idle TCP connection stalled every other client (a GET timed out at 5 s; answered at once when it closed); `Content-Length: 18446744073709551615` crashed the server (`lib.rs:445`; the next connect refused) — unauthenticated. A program printed under a console grant while `check` said `main` never performed `Write`, `authority` listed the printing function as pure, and `forge` made a `Cap[Console]` of an `Int` (DL0907 at run time). Actor generics had the same flaw. | 4.82 | ~~**A top-level constant's value was never checked**~~ (CONST-UNCHECKED-1; found 2026-10-24 by the agent test campaign's zero-authority agent, Haiku 5.5; HIGH). An unannotated constant was given a fresh type at each use, so `let X = 1` then `X + "a"` checked, and a constant holding a function was "not callable" (DL0404). | **Closed 2026-10-24** | `delulu-check/tests/constants.rs` (five tests: declared type, scope, purity, one type per use, honest constants; red before the fix). | 4.92 | **A poisoned device lock would stop the dead-man watchdog** (defensive; no trigger found). The watchdog takes `inner.leases.lock().unwrap()` on every tick (`device.rs`; 18 such unwraps in production device code), and the release profile unwinds on panic, so a panic under a device lock would poison it and end the watchdog. No production code that runs under those locks can panic: `sim.rs`, `adapter.rs`, `verified_adapter.rs` and `device.rs` hold no `unwrap`, `expect`, `panic!` or bare index outside the lock calls. | **Candidate — hardening** | Claude's cloud review, 2026-11-04 (source read; the review's §6.3, F7), downgraded in its second pass. A program that runs a Contained plugin through the WASM engine (R-1, `r_get_contained`) is future work, not a defect. |
| 4.97 | ~~**`verify-sig`, `sign` and `keygen` ignore an option they do not know, so a mistyped key pin fails open.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-11-06 by on-demand routine run 20 (D-V2-212).** The class is six commands, every one that reads its own options (`signing.rs`): `keygen`, `sign`, `verify-sig`, `publish`, `add`, `login`. Witnessed on the binary before the fix: `verify-sig art.dwx --kye K` dropped the pin and said *valid* (exit 0) where `--key K` says *invalid* (exit 1); `sign art.dwx --name second` signed with the default key; `sign --bogus` exit 0; `keygen --bogus` in a fresh home minted a key; `login … --bogus` stored a credential. **Now** the dispatcher refuses, for each of them, every `--option` (or `--option=value`) its usage does not document, before it runs: exit 2, *"`verify-sig` does not know this option: --kye"*, and with `--json` one envelope with `summary.errors` 1. The usage names the options the code already read and the help did not: `sign --key-name`, `publish --registry`, `keygen --dangerously-allow-insecure-perms` — so `toolchain --json` lists them — and `verify-sig`'s note no longer names `--hybrid`, which it never read (a note's `--word` is a documented flag). | **Closed** | `crates/delulu/tests/signing_cli.rs` `a_signing_command_refuses_an_option_it_does_not_know` (every spelling and mode of the mistyped pin; `sign --name`; `--bogus` on all six, writing nothing; the documented `--key`, `--key=HEX` and `--key-name` still working). Mutants M1108 (no guard), M1109 (`--x=v` read whole) and M1110 (three commands, not six) red. | Found by the hidden-complexity audit's second pass (its code-review sub-agent's F3). |
| 4.98 | ~~**A beat stamped in time and handed over late does not keep its dead-man.**~~ The daemon reads each request on a reader thread of its own, stamps it, and only then hands it to the serve loop (`brokerd.rs`, the reader in `serve_inner`); the loop judges each dead-man as of the earliest request already handed over, or as of now (DEADMAN-BUSY-1, D-V2-127). A beat stamped before its deadline and handed over after the loop judged is not counted: the dead-man fires, and the beat is answered with the revocation (DL1403). RW 4.87 named the window (*"one reader thread starved while the rest of the daemon runs"*) and took it to be microseconds; a starved reader makes it as long as the starvation. A false quarantine is the safe direction, but it revokes the monitor and every run under it. Whether RW 7.25's two reds were this is not known. | **Closed 2026-11-05 (D-V2-209)** — a beat read whole is stamped and noted under the lock the loop judges under, and counted only through `Broker::deadman_heard` (its key, its arrival in time, one period from when it was heard) | `brokerd.rs` `a_beat_stamped_in_time_but_handed_over_late_keeps_its_dead_man` (red 10 of 10 before D-V2-209 at its dead-man assertion, on the laptop, Windows, 2026-11-05; green since): a test-only seam (`handover_seam`, `#[cfg(test)]`, absent from the daemon a user runs) holds the reader of a beat stamped about 1.5 s into a 2 s period past the first deadline, and the chain is read at 2.4 s; the dead-man had fired, and the beat was answered DL1403. Its twin `a_beat_late_in_its_period_handed_over_at_once_keeps_its_dead_man_past_the_first_deadline`, the same beat handed over at once, is green (6 of 6), so the witness can pass and only the hold reddens it. (Its first form stamped the beat 0.7 ms in and read at 2.8 s, which a correct daemon fails too: a beat then extends the dead-man only to about 2 s. Corrected before it landed.) Its control `the_hand_over_seam_holds_one_beat_of_one_daemon` (M1080). Timing for the next red, both halves (`a_watch_held_outside_the_daemon_lets_its_dead_man_fire`, M1076–M1079): `broker.log` names a fired dead-man's last beats (stamped, taken, answered), what was waiting and how many connections a reader held, and a reader's delay in stamping or handing over of 100 ms or more; the monitor's standard error names its last beats (sent, by whom, answered in) and its longest turn. **Amended 2026-11-05, after an independent review of #131 and #132 (its four findings, each confirmed in the source first):** (1) the loop kept timing for any node a beat named, so beats naming made-up nodes grew it without end; now only an armed dead-man's beats are kept, the rest pruned, at most 256 nodes (`beats_naming_no_armed_dead_man_leave_no_timing_behind`: red before, 301 nodes kept); (2) a reader noted a beat before the protocol-version gate, so beats refused DL1406 that carried the right key kept a dead-man alive; now only a beat of this version is noted (`beats_of_another_protocol_version_do_not_hold_a_silent_dead_man_off`: red before, 92 such beats held a 300 ms dead-man off for 2 s); (3) a beat's *answered* time was taken before its reply was written; now *handled* and *written* (or *not written*, with why) are apart; (4) the monitor noted a beat only once answered, and printed its progress after joining a beater that can wait out a 15 s request; now each beat is noted when sent (*no answer yet*), and the progress is printed first. Mutants M1086-M1090 red. Both orders are witnessed since (the same day): a test-only seam holds a beat's answer 300 ms before it is written, and what the loop keeps must show the gap (`a_beats_answer_is_timed_when_it_is_written`; M1091, the write timed at handling, red: *"0ns apart"*); the watch's end is one function, `end_the_watch`, whose progress must be said while a beater still blocks (`a_watch_says_its_progress_before_it_waits_for_a_stuck_beater`; M1092, the join first, red). **Miri:** `miri-slow (delulu-broker)` in run `42093191962` on `master` `31b1692` (dispatched), 222 passed, 0 failed, 1 h 25 min, `deadman_heard`'s unit test among them. The daemon's side (`brokerd.rs`, in `delulu`) is not under Miri. | Done (D-V2-209). Counter-tests, each a 300 ms dead-man firing while the traffic still came: `incomplete_connections_do_not_hold_a_silent_dead_man_off` (20 connections, each two bytes of a frame), `beats_with_a_wrong_key_do_not_hold_a_silent_dead_man_off`, and `a_busy_daemon_still_fires_an_unbeaten_dead_man`; `delulu-broker` `a_beat_heard_in_time_counts_at_the_judgment_and_only_as_far_as_it_bought`; `the_beats_noted_between_judgments_are_bounded`. Mutants M1081 (the withdrawn fix: no judgment while any reader is active — the incomplete-connections test red), M1082 (a note counted without its key), M1083 (notes not applied), M1084 (two periods bought), M1085 (a late note counted), each red. Not covered: delays before a beat is read whole (a connection waiting to be accepted, a reader not yet run); the monitor's own timing shows that side. |

## 5. Proof and verification

| # | Item | Status | Verified | What closing it takes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | ~~**Delulu Core §1–§7 is not mechanized.** No capabilities, no store, no secrets, no attenuation, **no Progress and no Preservation**. What *is* machine-checked is the higher-order fragment only.~~ **Since 2026-10-22 (D-V2-175):** Theorems 1–3 — Progress-or-fault, Preservation, Effect Soundness — plus safety along a run, attenuation's monotonicity and the No-forgery corollary are machine-checked for the **monomorphic, closed-row fragment** of §1–§4 with capabilities, the store, attenuation, faults and secrets (`docs/design/models/lean/DeluluCoreSafety.lean`, Lean 4.32.2, no `sorry`, on `propext`/`Classical.choice`/`Quot.sound`; CI fails on any `sorry` and requires three mutants to fail). Still unmechanized: **type variables and open rows** (§5) and `Σ`'s internals (assumed well typed, `SigOk`). Mechanizing found `DELULU_CORE.md`'s `T-Verify` stale against the implementation (no label, where P17-IF1 gave it `Declassify`), `E-Expose` never written, and attenuation relied on but never a term: v0.4 states all three, and with them that `verify`'s label is the one no capability witnesses (2.6). | **Partial — the monomorphic fragment machine-checked, attenuation included (2026-10-22); type variables open** | `DELULU_CORE.md` §9; `MATHEMATICS.md` §12 category 2. Lean 4.32.2, `#print axioms` reports no axioms at all for all three theorems — genuinely strong, and genuinely narrow. | A ~500-line Lean or Coq development. `CONTRIBUTING.md` records it as open, invited work, and `CHECKPOINT-1.0.md` §9.2 ranks it second on the roadmap. |
| 5.2 | ~~**Theorem 1 (Progress) is FALSE as stated** and must be restated before it can be mechanized.~~ `E-Op` carried *"scope of κ permits the arguments"* as a premise, so a well-typed closed term with a present-but-too-narrow capability was **stuck** — which §7 forbade. | **Closed (2026-09-28, the first cloud routine run, D-V2-49) — restated as progress-or-fault in `DELULU_CORE.md` v0.3** | `DELULU_CORE.md` §9's second box. Observed: a program granted `fs.read=./data` reading `../outside.txt` checks clean and faults at run time with `DL0904`. The document contains no fault configuration at all. **Restated:** v0.3 adds the configuration `fault(c)`, `E-Refuse` (a present capability whose scope does not cover its arguments → `fault(scope)`, emitting the attempted label, as the runtime's trace does — witnessed) and `E-Fault` (no handler catches it); Theorem 1 is now *a value, a step, or a fault*, and Preservation and Theorem 3 are stated over faults. Recorded in `ENTRENCHED_CHANGE_RECORD.md` under the owner's delegation, flagged for his review. | Paper only, like the rest of §1–§7: the mechanization (5.1) is what makes it more than a sketch. |
| 5.3 | ~~**The calculus models no primitive that invokes a function argument**~~, so mechanizing §1–§7 *as written* would not have caught C88 — the worst soundness hole the project has had. | **Closed (2026-09-28, D-V2-49) — `DELULU_CORE.md` v0.3 has `hop`, `T-HOp`, `E-HOp`** | `DELULU_CORE.md` §9's first box: zero occurrences of `higher-order`, `callback`, `invoke` or `map` in §1–§7. **Extended:** `hop(e, ē)` invokes its function argument; `T-HOp` joins the callback's latent row (R-4) — the Lean fragment's `TypedGood.ho`, generalized from a literal closure to any term of arrow type; `E-HOp` unfolds to applications of the callback. Witnessed in the checker first: a pure-declared function passing a `! {Write}` callback to `List.map` is refused `DL0501`. | Mechanize it with the rest (5.1). |
| 5.4 | ~~**One `cargo-fuzz` target is written; the rest are not, and sanitizers are still unexercised.**~~ | **Closed (V2 P7, 2026-09-27)** | Six targets now (`fuzz/fuzz_targets/`): `channel_frame` (PS-A-02) and, for the parsers of bytes an attacker controls, `source` (program text through the whole checker), `manifest` (`delulu.toml`, a plugin manifest, a lockfile), `dir` (a `.dpx`'s compiled DIR: hostile CBOR never panics, and what decodes re-encodes canonically), `lease_token` (a broker that minted nothing redeems nothing) and `grant` (the `--grant` parser). Each property is ONE function in its crate (`delulu_check::fuzz`, `delulu_broker::lease::fuzz_one_lease_token`, `delulu_runtime::broker::fuzz_one_grant`) that the ordinary suite also replays over a seeded, deterministic mutation corpus (1,500–2,000 inputs each) on every commit. CI's `fuzz` job runs the channel for 60 s and each parser target for 30 s, coverage-guided under AddressSanitizer (`--sanitizer address`, named in the command). | Longer campaigns are the nightly's to schedule; MemorySanitizer needs an instrumented std and is not wired. |
| 5.5 | **Federation is not model-checked** — nor are concurrency, partitions, or clock skew. That last one matters, because the uplink lease exists to bound behaviour during exactly a partition. | **Open** | `QUESTIONS.md` Part 5 item 3. Leases and certificate adoption **are** model-checked (`Custody.tla`, 2,421 states), which is where both real federation vulnerabilities were found. | A TLA+ model of the federation protocol. The existing `Custody.tla` is the obvious starting point. |
| 5.6 | ~~**Three crates' Miri runs take hours on CI hardware, so they run nightly or by hand (*Run workflow* → `miri-slow`), not on every push — and 240 minutes is not enough for any of them either.**~~ | **Closed (2026-09-28): `miri-slow` `36381950975` on `047da1d` is green on all three crates** — `delulu-syntax` 20 min, `delulu-check` 58 min, `delulu-broker` 172 min, 0 UB, every test to the end within the 240-minute budget (read by the first cloud routine run) | CI run `34836508713` (2026-09-14, two vCPUs): each was cancelled at 45 minutes with 0 UB in what it had reached. `delulu-broker` stalled in `guard::tests::tier_for_subset_agrees_with_use_time_sealing_over_generated_policies`, `delulu-syntax` in `parser::tests::nesting_past_the_limit_is_refused_with_a_diagnostic_not_a_crash`, and `delulu-check` ran at about 30 s per test. None of the three contains `unsafe`; the FFI decoder, which does, passes on every push. **Measured again 2026-09-17** (run `35147900141`, public 4-CPU runners, 240 minutes each): all three cancelled at the budget, 0 UB — `delulu-broker` 124 of 150 (in flight: `path::tests::set_canonicalization_preserves_the_covered_region_on_generated_input`), `delulu-syntax` 104 of 131 (still inside `parser::tests::nesting_past_the_limit_is_refused_with_a_diagnostic_not_a_crash`, for over three hours), `delulu-check` 30 of 237 (in flight: `dir::tests::no_byte_flip_ever_verifies_with_altered_authority`). | Shrink those tests under `cfg!(miri)` as the bulk tests already do (the same code paths on a small input), then measure whether broker and syntax fit a push again. `delulu-check` at 30 s a test does not fit 45 minutes either way. Four CPUs changed little (Miri interprets on one host thread), so shrinking is the only route to a finished run; start with the three tests named in the Verified column. Until then every `miri-slow` job ends *cancelled*, including the nightly's, which runs it by default since 2026-09-17 — whether it stays in the nightly meanwhile is the owner's call. **2026-09-27 (V2 P7):** every test named above as stalled or in flight now scales under `cfg!(miri)` and keeps its full size natively — the Guard's generated policies (30,000 → 60), the nesting refusal (100,000 levels → just past the limit), the path canonicalization generator (3,000 → 30), both DIR flip sweeps (every byte → evenly spaced bytes, every bit), and the manifest's oversized name (200,000 → 2,000). Miri looks for undefined behaviour, and none of these sizes is about that; the sizes that ARE witnesses (the 100,000-level input) still run natively on every push. **Measured 2026-09-28** (run `36336888036`, on `4b583e4`): 0 UB; `delulu-syntax` FINISHED (130 passed, 1 ignored, 690 s); `delulu-check` 91 of 248, then its whole remaining budget in one generator (`rcaps::…criterion8_generated_sequences…`, 10,000 sequences) — shrunk to 20 under Miri, and its four per-row type-checking `prim_table` sweeps stride under Miri; `delulu-broker` 166 of 167 in 118 minutes, the one failure a real defect (AUDIT-LOCK-TAKEOVER-1, fixed — `V2_LOG.md` 2026-09-28). **Measured again 2026-09-28** (run `36368020490`, on `30a6b8d`): 0 UB, and every test in all three crates ran to the end inside the budget — syntax 130 passed, broker 168 passed in 176 minutes (the lock fix holds), check 247 of 248, its one failure a wall-clock bound Miri cannot judge, now ignored under Miri only. The next green `miri-slow` run closes this item. **2026-11-02 (on-demand routine run 19):** the broker's job ran 3 h 11 min of its 240 on `41383380395` (1 h 44 min the run before, on the same code — the runner moved), 8,119 s of it in `path::tests::canonicalization_laws_hold_on_generated_paths`; that test and the module's two corpus-pair tests now do their work once (`04db8fb`: the same inputs and laws, nothing shrunk under Miri; measured in the VM on a copy, 2.5x and 1.9x faster), and `728a582`'s `everything` `41416143488`, the first runner to read it, ran the job in 2 h 01 min — the laws test 4,191 s, the crate's tests 7,190 s, on a runner as slow as the last. |

| 6.13 | ~~**A filesystem miss does not say which capability scope it was looking in.**~~ **Closed 2026-09-18 (P1-F5), the cheap honest option:** under `--trace-effects` every filesystem record (both engines) carries `resolved_path` and `scope_root`, additive and machine-only; `IoErr` is unchanged. Test: `crates/delulu/tests/cli.rs` `a_filesystem_trace_record_names_the_resolved_path_and_the_scope_root`. A path passed through a `Cap[FsRead]` is relative to the capability's own root, and getting it wrong yields a bare `Err(IoErr::NotFound)` — no scope, no resolved path, nothing that distinguishes "the file is not there" from "you spelled the path as if it were relative to the working directory". | **Closed** | `prim.rs`: `io_err("NotFound")` carries no payload, and `IoErr`'s variants are part of the accepted language — giving `NotFound` a field would change every `match` over it, so it is **not** a message-only change and was left out of P1 deliberately. The rule itself is now stated in `delulu explain E-DL0703`, `docs/for-agents.md` [agents.cap-paths] and the Book Ch. 5, and `crates/delulu/tests/examples_run.rs` asserts the guide corpus's reads SUCCEED. | An RFC-shaped decision: either a new `IoErr` variant carrying the scope (language-visible, so an RFC), or a detail on the existing `Other(Str)` channel for this case only (which would reclassify a not-found as an other-error and is worse), or a `--trace-effects` field naming the resolved path (additive, machine-only, and the cheapest honest option). |

Measured on the laptop, 372,300 words into a `Map[Str, Int]` of 38: the counting loop 1.28–1.32 s → 0.29 s on WASM; the interpreter 0.63–0.80 s | The keys are compared where they lie: UTF-8 by its bytes (`str`'s order), anything else as its lossy reading compared, so every answer is the one the copies gave. |
| 6.37 | ~~**A `let` with a written type was read-only, and the refusal did not say why**~~ (found 2026-10-24 by the agent test campaign's zero-authority agent, Haiku 5.5, C6; met again by the laptop while measuring A5): `let b: List[Int] = []` then `b.push(2)` was DL1604 *"cannot write a list element (push) through a `val` receiver"*, though nothing in the program said `val`. A written type with no capability keyword is `val` (spec §2's default), where `let b = []` is `ref`; A12's own hint suggested `let m: Map[…] = Map()` and so led to it. | **Closed 2026-10-29** | `delulu-check` `campaign_wording` `a_write_refused_through_a_written_type_names_the_type` (red before; five shapes, the explicit `val` and a later `val` call among them); mutants M905–M907; `unknown_receiver` checks the hint; `cargo test -p delulu-check -p delulu-diag` and `core_invariance` passed (the snapshot unchanged) | The default is unchanged: it is the language's rule, and a `val` binding is what makes the lift at a call sound. DL1604 says that the written type made the binding `val`, labels the type, and gives the two remedies (`ref`, or no type). A1's lift message is kept for a binding the call made `val`; one already `val` by its type keeps the type as its reason. A12's hint writes `ref`. `GETTING_STARTED.md` §3 and §10 and `delulu explain DL1604` say it. |
| 6.38 | ~~**A fault on the WASM engine named no place, and its code twice**~~ (found 2026-10-24 by the agent test campaign's least-authority agent, Sonnet 5.5, B5): a lease refusal on `--engine wasm` was *"error[DL0904]: WASM engine: WASM trap: DL0904: lease … does not grant …"* with no `-->` line, where the interpreter pointed at the call. Every runtime fault on the WASM engine was so, a trap in the guest or a host refusal. | **Closed 2026-10-31** | `fault_parity` `a_wasm_fault_is_placed_where_the_interpreter_places_it` (eight shapes, each held to the interpreter's span), `a_recursion_too_deep_is_placed_at_the_call_that_went_deeper`, `a_refusal_a_check_records_is_placed_at_the_call_it_refused`; `wasm_cli` `a_wasm_fault_reads_as_the_interpreters_at_the_same_place` (red before); `broker_cli` `a_post_is_judged_by_its_method_under_the_daemon_as_embedded` (placed under either custody); mutants M916–M924 and M919 against the daemon | The compiler keeps a site map beside the module: each body's byte offsets, and the innermost statement or expression compiled from each. A refused host call records the guest's frames (every refusal goes through `Refuse::refuse`; the custody and declared-effect checks are asked through the caller), a trap carries its backtrace, and the CLI reads the innermost frame the map knows. The words are the interpreter's, the code once. The interpreter places an index fault at the whole `xs[i]`. The map is not in the module: 11 `.dwx` builds were byte-identical before and after. |
| 6.39 | ~~**A sandboxed run's fault names no place on the terminal**~~ (seen 2026-10-31 while closing B5, RW 6.38): under `--sandbox` the guest printed *"error[DL0904]: …"* alone, relayed as `guest: error[DL0904]: …`. The guest holds the program and the fault's span, but the channel's `Program` carried no file name. The run report already placed the host's refusal (P9-03). | **Closed 2026-10-31** | `sandbox_run_cli` `a_sandboxed_guests_fault_names_its_place_and_never_the_hosts_directories` (red before; a program named by its absolute path: placed at `share.delulu:5:16`, no guest line naming its directory); `broker_cli` `a_post_is_judged_by_its_method_under_the_daemon_as_embedded` checks the sandboxed route's place too; mutants M943–M945; `broker_cli`, `terminal_text_cli`, `sandbox_run_cli`, `sandbox_modes_cli`, `sandbox_guard_e2e` passed | `Program` gains `name` (additive, `#[serde(default)]`; host and guest are one version, D-V2-56): the file's last component, never its directories. The guest renders its fault at its span under that name; an empty name keeps the one-line form. |
| 6.40 | ~~**Two engine-parity gaps in what a fault reports**~~ (seen 2026-10-31 while closing B5): an actor that died on the WASM engine was counted (*"actors: 1 died"*) but not named, where the interpreter printed *"actor Div died in `go`: division by zero [DL0902]"*; and the interpreter's `DL0905` named no place, where the WASM engine named the call that went one deeper. | 6.73 | ~~**`run` refuses a missing grant only when the program reaches it, one at a time, after earlier effects have happened.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-11-06 by the laptop (D-V2-217).** Both remedies: (1) additive — a DL0703 refusal names every grant the program's text asks for that the run lacks (`cli::lacking_grants`: `authority --within` asked of the run's own final grants), as `missing_grants` on the `--json` envelope and in the run report, as a secondary span at each other call, and as one line of flags after the human rendering; on the interpreter and on `--engine wasm`. (2) Opt-in — `run --preflight` decides the text against those grants before `main` starts, on the ordinary run and under `--sandbox` (before a guest exists): a lacking grant is DL0703 naming every one, nothing runs, `outcome.ran: false`. A computed scope stays lazy: decided at its call against a grant of its kind, refused up front by its placeholder against none. The default stays lazy (a branch that never runs needs no grant); a `.dwx` refuses `--preflight` by name. Not done: under `--sandbox` a refusal mid-run still reaches neither the envelope nor the report (RW 6.75), and a sandboxed run refused before its guest starts writes no report (RW 6.83). The report still does not say what was done before a refusal. Was: `authority` lists every statically known grant (`required_grants`), but `run` grants at each use. `partial.delulu`, run with `--grant fs.write=./out` and not `console`, wrote `out/step1.txt` and then stopped with DL0703 for `console`; run with no grant, it named only `fs.write`. An agent that has not asked `authority` first pays one run per missing grant and may leave partial effects, and the run report does not say what was done before the refusal. | **Closed** (decided: D-V2-217) | `crates/delulu/tests/cli_contract.rs` `a_refusal_for_one_grant_names_every_grant_the_text_shows_the_run_lacks` (the audit's `partial.delulu` with no grant: one envelope, `missing_grants: ["fs.write=./out", "console"]`, the report the same, the other call placed; on `--engine wasm` the same, its report now recording the DL0703 as the interpreter's did; one grant given: the file still written, the default lazy) and `preflight_refuses_before_any_effect_naming_every_missing_grant` (no file, `outcome.ran: false`, `missing_grants: ["console"]`; under `--sandbox` both; a computed scope runs inside a grant of its kind and is refused up front, `fs.write=PATH`, against none). Both red on the old code (`missing_grants` absent; `--preflight` refused as an unknown option, exit 2). Mutants M1148–M1155 all red; control green. Found: the hidden-complexity audit, 2026-11-05 (the release build of `0670e0b` with its commit injected, SHA-256 `751c6458…`, on Linux x86-64; `docs/review/DeluluLang_Hidden_Complexity_AI_First_Experience_Audit.md` §5.3). | Done: the additive half for every run that is not sandboxed, and `--preflight` opt-in (the audit's §9 question 1, recommendation followed; whether it becomes the default for `--sandbox` runs is left open). |
| 6.74 | ~~**MCP cannot cancel a call, and one call holds up the next.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-11-06 by the laptop (D-V2-218).** The reader hands each `tools/call` to a pool of four workers (`mcp.rs` `Calls`) and answers `ping`, `initialize` and `tools/list` itself, at once. `notifications/cancelled` sets the named call's flag: a queued call is skipped, a running one's child is killed (`run_self_until`), and neither is answered. A Survey tool runs in-process to its end, its answer unsent. A second call with an id already in flight is refused. At the input's end every call still queued or running is answered before the server exits. Each tool answering with the CLI's envelope declares `outputSchema`: the `envelope` document, naming its payload where a closed schema describes it (`authority`, `atlas`, `chain`, `policy`, `toolchain`). The Survey tools declare none. Found on the way: the closed `chain` schema did not describe `capabilities[].requested_computed`, so `atlas chain` of any program with a capability failed `schema validate chain`. It is described now, and `schema_cli` validates the chain of every corpus program. Was: `run_mcp` reads and answers one line at a time (`crates/delulu/src/mcp.rs` lines 455–505) and drops every notification (line 539), `notifications/cancelled` included. A `check` cancelled 50 ms into its 0.5 s still answered with a result, and a `ping` sent behind it waited for it. The specification says a server SHOULD stop a cancelled request and not answer it (`2025-06-18` and `2026-07-28` alike). No tool declares an `outputSchema`, though `delulu schema` holds each command's closed schema. | **Closed** | `crates/delulu/tests/mcp_cli.rs` `a_cancelled_call_is_not_answered_and_a_ping_behind_a_call_is_answered_first` (a `check` of 16,000 functions, seconds long: a ping behind it is answered first; cancelled, the call gets no reply and the session ends in under half the control's time on the same machine, 3.2 s for the control on the laptop) and `each_tools_output_schema_is_the_clis_own_and_its_answer_conforms` (every CLI tool declares the envelope document, the Survey tools none; `authority`, `toolchain`, `check` and `atlas_chain` answers valid by `delulu schema validate`, envelope and payload). Both red on the old server: the ping waited behind the call; no `outputSchema`. `schema_cli` `the_authority_report_and_the_atlas_match_their_schemas` now also validates the chain, red before the schema fix (`/capabilities/0/requested_computed: a field the schema does not describe`). Mutants M1156–M1161 all red: no kill, a cancelled call answered, a cancel ignored, calls run on the reader, no `outputSchema`, no payload. M1156 by the timing alone: 3.19 s cancelled against the control's 3.22 s. Control green. Found: the hidden-complexity audit, 2026-11-05 (the release build of `0670e0b` with its commit injected, SHA-256 `751c6458…`, on Linux x86-64; `docs/review/DeluluLang_Hidden_Complexity_AI_First_Experience_Audit.md` §5.7). | Done as the row said; the process per call stays, and cancellation is a `kill`. The answer's size bound is RW 6.62. |
| 6.75 | ~~**A sandboxed run's report and envelope carry no diagnostic for a fault in the guest.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-11-06 by the laptop (D-V2-219).** The guest's goodbye carries the fault its program ended at (`channel.rs` `GuestFault`, an optional field of `Done`, not written when absent, so the protocol version and a faultless goodbye's bytes are unchanged). The host bounds its words as they arrive. `guest.rs` `run_diagnostics` then reports it in the `--json` envelope and the report's `diagnostics`, under three checks: only a registered code, a span placed only inside the program sent, and the label *"as the guest reports it"*. The host's own refusal wins when the guest's fault names the same code or none. A sandboxed DL0703 also carries `missing_grants` in both (RW 6.73's sandboxed half). Not done: a guest that ends without a goodbye (a signal, a closed channel) as its own outcome, RW 6.84. Was: `fault.delulu` (`1 / 0` through a function), run with `--json --report-out`, gives `DL0902` in the standard-output envelope and in the report's `diagnostics`. With `--sandbox`, both say `diagnostics: []`, `summary.errors: 1`, `outcome {exit 1, ran: true}`, and the code appears only on the guest's stderr. The same holds for DL0905 under `contained`, and for a guest that aborted (row 3.29). So under the sandbox an agent cannot tell a fault, its code or its span from any other exit 1 without parsing human text. | **Closed** (its abnormal-end half is RW 6.84) | `crates/delulu/tests/sandbox_run_cli.rs` `a_guests_own_fault_reaches_the_envelope_and_the_report` (the row's `fault.delulu` under `--sandbox --json --report-out`: `DL0902` at line 4 in the envelope and in the report, labelled as the guest's) and `a_sandboxed_refusal_names_every_grant_the_run_lacked` (DL0703, *"refused by the host"*, `missing_grants` in both). Both were red on the old code (`diagnostics: []`, the bare *"the command ran and failed"*). Unit tests: `guest.rs` `a_guests_fault_is_reported_only_as_a_registered_code_inside_the_program` (precedence; an unregistered code and a forged word dropped; spans outside the program, backwards or in another file unplaced; a clean goodbye records none) and `channel.rs` `a_goodbye_carries_the_guests_fault_bounded_and_without_one_is_unchanged` (bounded and escaped; a faultless goodbye byte-identical to the old encoding, which reads back as one). A guest killed by a signal, or one whose channel closed, sends none: the host says *"the guest closed the channel without saying goodbye"* on stderr, and the envelope and report say exit 1 with an empty `diagnostics` and no field naming the end. ## 7. Platform, distribution and process

| # | Item | Status | Verified | What closing it takes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | ~~**macOS has never been executed.**~~ **Verified by CI on 2026-09-14:** run 3 passed the whole suite on the macOS runner — 1,657 tests, 0 failed — and every gate after it (`CROSS_PLATFORM_VERIFICATION.md` §9). | **Closed** | Re-measured 2026-08-10: `delulu-diag`, `delulu-syntax`, `delulu-measure`, `delulu-survey` type-check for `aarch64-apple-darwin`; the rest stop in third-party C build scripts (`blake3`, `zstd-sys`, `libffi-sys`) *before* reaching DeluluLang code. So **no DeluluLang source has been shown to fail — and most has not been shown to compile either.** Exactly one line branches on macOS (`SUN_PATH_MAX` 104 vs 108). | **Closed by CI's macOS runner, three runs after the first push** — the default build included, CPython embedding and all, so `--no-default-features` is no longer the only macOS-safe path. What CI cannot close: a developer's Mac, and the VS Code extension on one. |
| 7.2 | ~~**CI has never executed.**~~ **Green on 2026-09-14:** run 5 (`34849980129`) passed every job — the whole suite on macOS, Linux x86-64, Linux arm64 and Windows in one run, 0 failed anywhere, and every gate after it (`CROSS_PLATFORM_VERIFICATION.md` §9). Until that day the repository was never pushed, so CI never ran; the YAML parsed, and every command in it was believed to have been run by hand — until the first run went red and the Miri command, as written, turned out unable to run in this tree (`HANDOFF.md` §1.1). | **Closed** | The owner had the repository pushed to a private testing remote on 2026-09-14 (`HANDOFF.md` §1.1), which activates `ci.yml` on every push to `master`, on pull requests and by hand (nightly only where the `NIGHTLY` variable is on). So it is the third dead probe: a daemon slower than DEADMAN-1's 1 s, which RW 4.53 measured at a 1.077 s maximum beside busy writers. The fail-safe is by design; the cost is that a run's own audit traffic, on a loaded host, can park its own machine. Fix direction, not yet taken (a safety trade-off — the deadline is how fast a hung broker parks a device): answer liveness off the daemon's serial queue, or judge a probe by when the daemon took it, as DEADMAN-BUSY-1 judges a beat. **Neither is safe as stated** (the same night, on reflection): a probe answered off the queue while the handler is stuck would keep saying "live" from a tree no revoke can reach — an operator's e-stop queued behind the stuck handler would never land, and the device would keep moving, where today a stuck handler fails safe (the probe times out, the device parks); and the client cannot see when the server took its probe. An off-queue answer would have to prove the handler is making progress. The failed job rerun on the same commit (`39672085470` attempt 2) passed — the timing class, not the change. The honest next step is a measurement: the daemon's per-request latency on `windows-latest` beside a run's own appends, and whether the 1 s deadline or the appends' `fsync` is what to change. **The instrument (2026-10-22, evening):** `broker.log` names every answer that left 500 ms or more after its request arrived, with its kind, its wait for the requests ahead of it and its own time (`broker_cli` `an_answer_given_late_is_named_in_the_broker_log`, three mutants red); `estop_cli`'s two no-revoke controls attach the log to a failure and print each late answer on a green run, so the measurement is `witness.yml` on `windows-latest` at `estop_cli`. **Read (2026-10-22):** runs `39810107555` and `39810113455` at `86b8df5`, all green — in the four no-revoke control runs no answer was late (none ≥ 500 ms). | 7.25 | **`monitor_cli`'s `a_long_chain_does_not_kill_a_healthy_monitor` failed once on a macOS runner** — `master`'s push run `40793268946` on `31d26bc` (2026-10-29, job `123183433447`; the same commit's pull-request run `40791344842` and the next push runs green): six periods into the watch, the monitor and its run were revoked at the record after the test's delegation. The message held only the nodes' states, so it could not tell the monitor not scheduled for a period (the dead-man's purpose), the monitor stopped on an error (its dead-man left armed, by design), or the daemon not running. **Red again 2026-11-05, on Windows:** `master`'s push run `42015437785` on `5fb865c` (job `127204705719`; the same commit's pull-request run `42012298873` was green). It failed at the first check, four periods (2 s) into the watch. **What its evidence shows:** the broker revoked the monitor after five accepted beats (*"no beat within 500 ms (armed at seq 101256, 5 beat(s))"*); `broker.log` names no deaf interval while the dead-man was armed; and the test's own `grants list` (a `ListPage`, from `node_states`) was answered 1,881 ms after its reader stamped it, 2 ms of that handling it. The failed job's re-run, attempt 2 (job `127217198088`), passed, as the macOS one's next runs had. **What it does not show** (corrected 2026-11-05 on the laptop, after an independent read of the source and the job log; this row had said the monitor was still running, that the daemon was hearing, and that the cause was narrowed to a broker queue): the monitor's exit status `None`, read after it had printed *"ended"*, does not exclude an earlier pause of the monitor, and what kept it alive after that line is unmeasured; five accepted beats are a count, not a time, and do not say when the last one arrived; the `ListPage`'s delay is its stamp-to-handler time, which holds its reader's hand-over and any stall of the handler together, so it measures delayed processing of that one request, not a queue, not its cause, and not its order relative to the firing. Monitor-side delay (its beater beats only while it waits on the broker, `monitor_cmd.rs`) and broker-side delay in receiving or handing over a beat both remain possible, and the two failures need not share a cause. One broker-side path is known from source: a reader stamps a beat and only then hands it to the handler, and a handler that judges a dead-man in between does not count it (RW 4.87's *"one reader thread starved while the rest of the daemon runs"*, there assumed to be microseconds). That path is RW 4.98, witnessed red and closed on 2026-11-05 (D-V2-209). Closing it does not say either red was it: a monitor-side delay, or a delay before a beat is read whole, remain possible. **Run six times more on 2026-11-05, with RW 4.98's timing in place** (`witness.yml` on `master` `31b1692`: Windows `42093261520`, `42093278446`, `42093295263`; macOS `42093310306`, `42093323509`, `42093337696`): six of six green, each log showing the test run and its measurement (67,501 to 101,251 records; one whole read 1.10 to 1.52 s). Not reproduced. **Re-run as it failed, 2026-11-06** (the owner: *"Why isn't it solved yet"*): those six ran the one test alone, while both reds came in push runs, where the whole `monitor_cli` target runs its tests side by side, each starting daemons and monitors, so they said little. `witness.yml` now takes `repeat` and `stress` (busy loops, one CPU each). The whole target on `master`'s code: Windows 20 runs (`42146850780`) and 12 beside three busy loops on its four CPUs (`42146857227`); macOS 20 (`42146863757`) and 12 beside two on its three (`42146870039`); 64 of 64 green. On the laptop (Windows, 16 CPUs, its real-time scanner on): 15 of 15, and 30 of 30 beside 16 busy loops. GitHub's Windows runners run with the real-time scanner off (`RealTimeProtectionEnabled: False`), so the scanner is not the Windows red's cause. A second round of about 1,100 runs (Windows `42148221551`, `42148230468`, and beside three busy loops `42148239197`, `42148250027`; macOS `42148266268`, `42148279292`, and beside two `42148287472`, `42148295822`) has one red so far, the rest read in the next entry. Two reds in some 330 push and pull-request runs since 2026-10-19, each on Windows and macOS, is a rate a few hundred runs may not reach. **Reproduced, 2026-11-06, on macOS:** `42148295822`, run 9 of 150 beside two busy loops on a five-CPU runner, revoked six periods into the watch (68 beats counted). The monitor's last kept beats were sent at +1960, +2004, +2004 and +2092 ms after its dead-man was armed, each answered in 0 ms; its next was sent at +3944 ms and refused. The daemon stamped, took, handled and answered each of the four in the same millisecond, and fired at its deadline. So the daemon lost and delayed nothing: the monitor sent no beat for 1,852 ms, and its beater, which beats only while the watch waits on the daemon, sent none either. The silence was the monitor's own: a step outside a request (its follower's read, its rules, its sleep), or the whole process not run. It began about 2.1 s into the watch, when the test's delegation is appended; the macOS red of 2026-10-29 was revoked right after that delegation too. Which step it was is named by the step timing of `d3da12b`, now running the same way (macOS `42153802328`, `42153809227`, `42153816861`; Windows `42153823661`, `42153830411`). **Read 2026-11-06 by on-demand routine run 20.** Round 2: Windows `42148221551` (150 of 150) and `42148250027` (100 of 100, three busy loops) green, macOS `42148266268` and `42148287472` green; **Windows `42148239197` red at run 60 of 100, beside three busy loops on four CPUs** (on `e4c94f8`, before the step timing) — the monitor's beats by the watch at +293, +298, +331, +340 and +372 ms, the next at +4110 (refused); its longest turn 4,894 ms, over a chain of 30,001 records whose one whole read took 1.05 s; `broker.log`: judged at +4032 ms, its last beats stamped +3456 to +3529, nothing waiting, and *"a ListPage request was answered 2113 ms after it arrived: 2110 ms waiting for the requests ahead of it"*. Windows `42148230468` was red in another test (RW 7.29). Round 3, on `d3da12b` (the step timing): macOS `42153802328`, `42153809227` and Windows `42153823661` green; macOS `42153816861` red at run 108 of 150 in RW 7.29's test, not this one; Windows `42153830411` cancelled at `witness.yml`'s 90-minute job limit, with no run red before it (its annotations hold only the limit) — a Windows repeat must fit 90 minutes, build included. So no red since the step timing landed has been this test's. | **Open — red twice (macOS 2026-10-29, Windows 2026-11-05), green on the next run each time; reproduced 2026-11-06 under load (macOS, 1 of 150 runs): the daemon heard every beat at once, and the monitor itself went silent for 1,852 ms. Which step held it is not yet named; one more red read 2026-11-06 (Windows, beside three busy loops, before the step timing)** | `crates/delulu/tests/monitor_cli.rs` `revoked_monitor_evidence`: the monitor's exit status and standard error, and `broker.log` (a dead-man's firing with its beats, every late answer, and the daemon's own deaf interval since RW 4.87) | Not reproduced in the VM: 3 of 3 green quiet, 6 of 6 beside four copies of the whole target. Not by lengthening the period, and a green re-run is not an answer. The witness of the stamp-to-hand-over window and the timing now exist (RW 4.98, 2026-11-05): the next red's job log names the monitor's last beats (sent, answered in) and its longest turn, and `broker.log` the daemon's last beats for that dead-man (stamped, taken, answered), what was waiting, and any reader slow to stamp or hand over. Read them before naming a cause. Since 2026-11-06 both lines are on one wall clock (the daemon names when it began serving, the monitor when its dead-man was armed, each in ms since the Unix epoch), and the monitor names its longest whole turn and the turn that ended the watch step by step, and its longest sleep between turns (M1093–M1098): one red names its path. |
| 7.26 | **An Ubuntu test job died of a full disk once** (RUNNER-DISK-1) — #101's pull-request run `41121497877` on `ff0b4f6` (2026-10-30, job `124288578335`; `6516ae5`'s pull-request and push runs, carrying the same code, green): 2 min 50 s in, about a minute into the suite, the runner wrote *"System.IO.IOException: No space left on device"* as its last words; the suite step has no conclusion and the log answers 404. Once in the 223 CI runs since 2026-10-20 (every failed job of them read by its annotations). So the disk was nearly full when the build began, after the cache restore, and nothing said how full. | **Closed 2026-10-31 (on-demand routine run 9) — measured: not the build's** | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: each test job and the arm64 job read the workspace disk's free space and `target/`'s size before the suite and after it, as notices (`RUNNER-DISK-1 — this runner's disk …`) | The first readings (`f5a1d58`'s push run `41164074066`): Ubuntu 82 GB free of 145 GB before the suite and 67 GB after (`target/` 2.9 → 18 GB); Windows 144 → 129 GB of 150; macOS 93 → 89 GiB of 320; arm64 104 → 91 GB of 145. The margin is wide on every runner, so that runner's disk was full for its own reason, not the suite's. The notices stay: a second death names its margin. **Seen a second time (read 2026-11-05 on the laptop):** the nightly `41619070519` on `e97c747` (2026-11-03, job `125859825495`) died 1 min 41 s in, 57 s into the suite, with the same last words; the nightly `41813603704` on the same commit the next day passed 18 of 18. The margin was NOT named: the before-suite step succeeded, but its notice died with the runner — the job's annotations hold the `IOException` alone and its log answers 404 (`BlobNotFound`). So a notice cannot record a death's margin; a reading that outlives the runner has to leave it before the suite (an uploaded artifact, say) — not built. |

The live runtime witness last ran on 2026-10-04 (`37234907676`); the prover job runs on pushes that touch the export, last on 2026-11-02 (`41394552103`). | **Open** | Claude's cloud review, 2026-11-04 (the review's §8.1, F11; OpenShell's releases page read that day). | Bump the pin, dispatch `openshell.yml` with the runtime witness, read it, and record what broke as upstream's; then the proof of concept in `docs/DELULULANG_V2/V2_OPENSHELL_STUDY.md` §8. |
| 7.29 | **`monitor_cli`'s `a_watch_held_outside_the_daemon_lets_its_dead_man_fire` is red in two shapes under repeated runs** (found 2026-11-06 by on-demand routine run 20, reading RW 7.25's repeat runs). **(1) Windows `42148230468`** (on `e4c94f8`), run 13 of 150, no busy loops: the monitor ended *"`g_…` was revoked — nothing left to watch"*, exit 0, where the test asks for a failure naming its own dead-man (`monitor_cli.rs` line 1694). By the code (`monitor_cmd.rs`, the beat's DL1403/DL1402 branch, and the state check before it), that ending is reached only when `read_chain().ok().and_then(\|recs\| deadman_fired_at(&recs, &node))` is `None`: the chain could not be read, or did not yet hold the dead-man's revocation at that read. In the first case an error reading the chain is taken for *someone else revoked it* — a clean end, exit 0 — which is fail-open for the monitor's outcome whatever this red's cause was. **(2) macOS `42153816861`** (on `d3da12b`), run 108 of 150 beside four busy loops: the monitor neither ended nor printed past its watching line within 27.3 s of a 732 ms hold, and the test killed it. The test's message assumes *"something beat for it outside the daemon"*; it prints no `broker.log` and no node state, so whether the dead-man fired is not known. **Shape 1's fail-open closed 2026-11-06, the same session, at the owner's word (*"CI Runs failed … What about that CI run fails"*; D-V2-213, `b1d7d44`):** a watch whose node is gone now ends clean only on a revocation the chain names — its own dead-man's record (a failure, as before), someone else's revocation recorded since the dead-man was armed, or a TTL's end. A chain it cannot read, or one that names no revocation since arming, ends the watch as a failure: *"`g_…` was revoked, and this monitor cannot tell by whom: … its dead-man may have fired"*. Witness `monitor_cmd::tests::a_gone_node_ends_the_watch_clean_only_on_a_revocation_the_chain_names`, red on the old ending (*"`g_m` was revoked — nothing left to watch"* for an unreadable chain). Mutants M1111–M1115 red (M1115 by the TTL test `a_watch_whose_node_expires_ends_clean_with_its_dead_man_armed`); **M1116** (a refused beat's DL1402 read as an expiry) survives: the TTL witness meets the expiry at the state check, and no test can place a TTL's end between a turn's state check and its beat — the mapping is the refusal code's own meaning (DL1402, *lease expired*). The full suite on `b1d7d44`: 192 targets, 2,693 passed, 0 failed; `witness.yml` Windows `42168550190` and macOS `42168552718` green, every new test named. **The test is instrumented:** each of its three failures now prints the node's state and `broker.log` (whether the daemon fired, and whether the chain took the record — RW 4.58's warning). | **Open — the fail-open closed; neither shape's cause is established** (shape 1's red now says whether the record was missing or the read failed; shape 2's says whether the dead-man fired) | the two job logs named (`127664802322`, `127683111437`), read by on-demand routine run 20 | Wait for the next red and read its `broker.log` before naming a cause. Not by lengthening the hold. |

The thread-count test counts only threads its own daemon made, or runs alone. |

## 8. If you are picking one thing

Ordered by value returned per unit of effort, which is **not** the order the roadmaps use (they
order by importance):

1. **Run `cargo test --workspace` on any Mac** (7.1). One command; converts the project's largest
   standing caveat from an argument into evidence. Nothing else about the third platform matters
   until this happens. Since 2026-09-14 CI's `macos-latest` job runs it on every push to the private
   testing remote (7.2), so this item is now *reading that result and recording it*, not finding a Mac.
   Run 1 stopped building `libffi-sys`'s bundled C; run 2, on macOS's own libffi, passed 1,654 of 1,655.
   **Done 2026-09-14:** run 3 was green on macOS end to end (7.1, closed).
2. **Wire the saved NIST KAT vectors into a test** (4.10a). The vectors are already in the tree.
   This is the only item here whose inputs are already committed.
3. **Write the `cargo-fuzz` targets** (5.4). Mechanical, independent of everything else, and it is
   what would move proof-boundary category 6 from *Partial* — where the differential harness runs
   but no coverage-guided target exists — to genuinely populated.
4. **Register the CLI strings** (6.1). Purely additive; turns a complete-but-empty mechanism into a
   working one and makes 6.2/6.3 possible.
5. **Grow the standard library** (2.1). The largest gap between what the language *is* and what a
   user expects. `filter` and `fold` are the two that make the higher-order machinery — already
   built and audited under R-4 — actually worth having.
6. ~~**Restate Progress as progress-or-fault** (5.2).~~ Done in `DELULU_CORE.md` v0.3 (2026-09-28,
   D-V2-49), with the higher-order primitive (5.3): the mechanization (5.1) now has the right target.
7. **The language basics (2.33–2.40). Added 2026-11-02, and now first by value — and FIRST in the work order since
   2026-11-06, at the owner's word (D-V2-220): built as surface syntax and prelude over the core, each followed by
   programs of varying size written, run and fixed (`docs/CLOUD_ROUTINE.md` step 4).** Nearly all are additive: a new
   operator, prelude function or method breaks no program that checks today, so any 2.x can carry them. But a public
   release is judged by the absence of `x += 1` and `abs`. Methods on one's own types come before interfaces, which
   need a typeclass design and an RFC (2.9); then the interpreter's compiled form (3.24). **Done:** compound assignment
   (2.34, 2026-11-08, D-V2-221; its programs found 2.45, 2.46 and 3.32, and fixed 3.31); 2.46 with 2.47 (2026-11-08,
   D-V2-222); math and the checked forms (2.38, 2026-11-09, D-V2-223; its programs found two formatter defects, fixed,
   and 2.48 and 2.49). **Next:** ranges (2.36), then strings (2.37).
8. **The ordinary agent utility, and the answers agents read (added 2026-11-05).** Two independent reviews put the same
   thing first: Codex's, and Claude's, which re-ran Codex's. An agent cannot yet write an ordinary utility, which needs:
   - its inputs and exit code (2.40);
   - JSON (2.41), math (2.38, built 2026-11-09), strings (2.37) and methods (2.33);
   - on WASM, the documented constructs (3.25).

   And an agent cannot always tell from a tool's answer whether its task succeeded: 6.51, 6.54–6.60, 4.88. Claude's
   review orders the work:
   1. the answer contracts first, each small and each witnessed red;
   2. the basics, as 2.0's bar;
   3. one capability matrix (6.61).

   Most of the basics change the frozen core, so the RFCs' 14-day comment periods (`CONTRIBUTING.md` §5) are the
   critical path, unless a recorded decision unfreezes the core for a named set. JSON, math and string functions are
   library-only and can start at once.

**What not to do first:** the optimizer (3.1) and the native backend (3.2). Both are deferred with
published reasoning, and ruling D18c's argument still holds — there is no evidence the passes would
pay off, and building an optimizer before you can measure it is how a deferral becomes a claim.

---

## 9. What this document is not

It is **not** a promise, a schedule, or a commitment. `CHECKPOINT-1.0.md` §9 puts it well: *"None
of that is a promise with a date. It is the list a maintainer would work from."*

It is also not a list of everything that could be better. It is the set of things that a document
in this repository **describes, specifies, or implies**, and that the code does not do — plus the
handful of places where the code has moved ahead of the documents (§1). Since 2026-11-02 it also holds what an
ordinary program needs and the language lacks (1.15, 2.33–2.40): no document described those, and every newcomer
meets them first.

**Related reading.** `docs/release/CHECKPOINT-1.0.md` §8 (twenty known limitations) and §9 (the
roadmap) · `docs/QUESTIONS.md` Part 5 (the short list of things this project cannot claim) ·
`docs/MATHEMATICS.md` §12 (the proof-boundary ledger, including the categories that are empty or
partial) · `docs/book/THE_DELULULANG_BOOK.md` Ch. 20 ("What actually remains") ·
`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` §5 (what is not protected) · `HANDOFF.md` §8 (what was open, and why — since
2026-09-27 in `docs/archive/v1/HANDOFF_HISTORY.md`, when V2 phase P6 made `HANDOFF.md` a briefing).

Those six are the sources. This file is the join across them, with every row re-checked against the
binary rather than inherited from the prose.
